# CatCode migration — STATUS dashboard

**This is the single source of truth for "what's done."** A session asked to report migration
progress reads THIS file first. When a session finishes a unit of work, it updates the row here
(status + date + one-line note) as its last step.

Status legend: ⬜ not started · 🟡 in progress · ✅ done · 🛑 blocked
Model tag: **CLAUDE** = visual-design / system-architecture judgment · **ANY** = model-agnostic.
Difficulty: 1–10 (operator picks reasoning effort from it).
**🖐 GUI** = stamped only on sessions whose verification needs the operator to drive a live
GUI/browser step (launch the Electron app, click, keypress); absent = fully headless-verifiable.

> **When a phase's sessions are all ✅, the next phase's backlog gets generated** (per
> PROGRAM-PLAN §8) — with the 🧠 Model/Difficulty tag stamped on every task it produces.
> Phases 4–5 are NOT split into sessions yet; they appear here only as coarse rows until opened.

**P4-8 desktop snapshot correction (2026-08-04):** the sidecar now reads the exact current engine session with `readSessionState(getSessionId())` and joins only live `AppState.tasks`; it no longer imports prior-session continuity workers. The engine's `readSessionStateWithContinuity` path remains unchanged for engine resume tools. Compact worker rows show genuine names only, normalized type text instead of the visible `Resumable` word, one leading role marker in the docked roster, and one lifecycle pip without a duplicate type ring; structural roster/Workers-dialog assertions close F1 from `reviews/2026-08-04-subagent-panel-session-scope-review.md` headlessly (**54/0 focused renderer tests**, renderer typecheck/build clean). The historical P4-8 row below is superseded on these seams; renderer GUI acceptance remains pending.

| **CC-60 · The rollup lane was being evicted by restarts, and a replay was stamped delivered before delivery was decided** → `app/main/deliveryTraceSink.ts`, `app/main/main.ts`, `app/preload/preload.ts` | ✅ **CODE+HEADLESS DONE 2026-08-15** | **Validation pass over `docs/reports/2026-08-14-desktop-logging-feedback.md`; dispositions for all eight of its items are recorded at the top of that report.** The find is that CC-52's rollup lane was correct and unreachable in practice. Lane files are named per launch, so a restart always opens a new one and `retain()` drops the oldest on `count > maxFiles` **before age or bytes are consulted**; at `MAX_DELIVERY_ROLLUP_FILES = 4` that made the 72h age cap unreachable. Measured on this machine: four rollup files totalling **79 KB against the 4 MB budget**, with the 2026-08-14 freeze onset already evicted by ordinary restarts the following day. CC-52's row says per-frame pressure provably cannot reach the lane, which held; restart pressure was the path nobody costed, and that row is left as written rather than rewritten. Cap raised to 128 so bytes and age govern, with the file cap bounding directory entries only. **This changes the standing verdict that the 2026-08-14 freeze onset was unrecoverable:** the lane carries per-stream per-minute watermarks, and a freeze is `applied` flat while `ipcSent`/`preloadReceived` climb, so onset was recorded and simply never read. The playbook in `docs/reports/2026-08-15-renderer-incidents-final.md` §7 now opens on `latest-delivery-rollup` before the per-frame trace. Also landed: **D1**, `attachment.replayed` was stamped inside the `.map()` over the gate's output while `deliver()` could still decline on four conditions, so the trace recorded deliveries that never happened and 1,883 of them were reported to the operator as a delivery pathology; the stage is now stamped only on frames handed to `webContents.send` via a shared `deliverableContents()` predicate, and an undeliverable replay writes its own `diagnostic` record instead of a silent absence. **A2** closed as documentation, not behavior: `eventLoopLagMs` measures timer scheduling correctly, and probe-time re-sampling was considered and rejected because the event loop was genuinely idle during the freeze and would have reported the same 4.5 ms; the constraint now sits at the definition in `preload.ts` and as a new corollary in `decisions/OBSERVABILITY-MINIMUM.md` §4. **Verification:** `deliveryTraceSink` + `mainSourceGuards` + `mainDecisions` + `attachmentGate` + `operationalLogSink` + `diagnosticsBundle` + `operationalLog` **144/0**, 2 new tests both shown failing before the change (the rollup test by flipping the constant back to 4; the D1 guard is an absence assertion in the file that documents why `main.ts` admits only those). `bun test app/` **3281/13**; app tsc clean; sidecar wrapper passed (5,580 upstream ignored); hardening **19/19**; `maps:lint` 18 maps, 7 pre-existing warnings. **The 13 are not ours and none are in touched code:** 11 credential-gated live-sidecar probes (the class CC-42 through CC-52 all recorded), plus two in `app/main/replayBuffer.test.ts` which fail in isolation on a clean tree because `fa3531c4` added `stats.usage.snapshot` to the attach burst without the sticky table, and `app/sidecar/turnLifecycle.test.ts` which is real-timer load-sensitive and measured at 7/0, 6/1, 7/0 across three isolated runs. No protocol, preload channel, inbound-frame, renderer, or locked-decision change; no user-visible surface. **§0 flags:** the per-frame lane's retention is untouched and remains the owner decision in `docs/reports/2026-08-10-delivery-trace-retention-measurement.md`; D2 (replay bursts unnamed in the log) is deliberately deferred behind the burst fix in `docs/plans/2026-08-14-renderer-freeze-fix-plan.md` rather than instrumenting a behavior about to be removed; the applied-versus-queued freeze detector stays an open decision beside the stream watchdog. | **CC-52 · The delivery trace needed a lane rotation cannot reach** → `app/main/deliveryTraceSink.ts`, `app/main/diagnosticsBundle.ts` | ✅ **CODE+HEADLESS DONE 2026-08-10** (`6bc3aac3`) | **CC-51's rollup recommendation, and only that one: per-frame tracing is untouched, and the two neighbouring options the operator declined (1-in-20 stage sampling, raising `MAX_DELIVERY_TRACE_TOTAL_BYTES`) are not implemented.** Per-frame detail is what fills the disk (679 B x ~12 records per frame, 9.85 MB/min under load, ~10 minutes of history in the 100 MB budget), and the facts an investigation opens first are stream-level and cheap. New `trace.stream.rollup`: one record per stream per sweep carrying the eight watermarks, the delivered-through verdict (`complete`/`incomplete`+stage/`unknown`, from the same `firstMissing(…, deliveredOnly)` CC-49 fixed, now a shared `deliveryVerdict` helper so the two records cannot drift), the last SDK message kind, `traceLossCount`, and one counter per anomaly kind that actually fired (absent when zero). **630 B per record measured** against real identifier lengths (not the ~520 B first estimated, corrected in `1db7965f`); **4 MB total cap against 100 MB today**, which covers the full existing 72h age cap for one continuously active stream (2.7 MB) and divides across concurrent ones, since the cap is a total. **Its own budget is the whole point, and it took two separate mechanisms:** the sink now writes through a small file-lane abstraction so the rollup gets its own files (`delivery-rollup-*`), its own rotation, and a retention pattern anchored on its own prefix — pressure on the per-frame files provably cannot reach it; AND its own admission reserve in the bundle (`MAX_BUNDLE_ROLLUP_BYTES`, 256 KiB), because separate files alone were not enough: one busy minute of per-frame records fills the general 1 MiB admission budget and the summaries were being squeezed out of every export, oldest-mtime-first. Allowlisted + validated in `parseDeliveryTraceRecord` (an unlisted kind is rejected whole and silently; the reject-side cases cover the incomplete/stage pairing, a path in a watermark, and a message kind outside the closed vocabulary). **Idle streams:** an interval that changed nothing writes nothing — the emitter fingerprints the state it just asserted (watermarks, verdict, anomaly counts, losses; deliberately NOT `quietMs`, which grows every interval by definition) and suppresses an unchanged repeat, so the count is bounded by the stream's work, never by how long a failure lasts (OBSERVABILITY-MINIMUM.md §5). Fingerprint is left unset on a failed write so the next interval retries rather than suppressing; the lane carries no `trace.loss` of its own because a lost summary is re-derivable from state still in memory. Rides CC-49's existing 60s sweep and its `close()` (after the quiescence sweep, so the last rollup carries the quiescence it just counted) rather than arming a second timer; it writes and returns, touching no socket and no session (§3). **Verification:** focused suites **39/0** (6 new). Because three of the six fail on the missing method rather than on behavior, each was additionally mutation-checked against post-change source: sharing the trace file prefix fails the retention test, deleting the fingerprint check fails the idle test, dropping the anomaly counters fails the facts test. `bun test app/` **3066/11** at the commit in a detached worktree, all 11 the known credential-blocked `*.probe.test.ts` live-engine suites (the exact set varies run to run by timeout, as CC-48 recorded); app tsc clean; sidecar wrapper passed (5,657 upstream ignored); hardening **19/19**. No protocol, preload, inbound-frame, renderer, or locked-decision change; no user-visible surface. **§0 flags / carry-forwards:** rollup records are NOT folded into `deriveStuckSessionSummaries`, which still reads only per-frame records, so when those have rotated away `stuckSessions` is empty and the equivalent per-stream facts live in `streams.deliveryRollup` instead; a reader that only knows the old field sees less than the bundle holds. The lane filename now uses the injected `now()` instead of `Date.now()`, so a caller-supplied clock owns rotation (needed for a deterministic rotation test). Retention is proven by construction and by test, not against a live 100 MB directory: the byte projection is arithmetic from CC-51's measurement, not a re-measurement. |

Two deliberate silences: a turn still producing messages re-arms instead of firing (long is not stalled), and a turn waiting on an unanswered permission is a designed wait whose pending request is held right here. Only `message` events count as liveness — `turn.status` is the controller's own bookkeeping and fires at both ends of every turn, so counting it would have reset the quiet clock for free. **A1 was filed as blocked on `app/sidecar/sessionController.ts`, which is still held by another session's uncommitted work; that file builds the engine config, and the turn boundary is `activeTurn` in `sidecarServer.ts`, which was free.** 7 tests, all shown failing against pre-change sources in a detached worktree. |

Lifecycle is a closed 32-event set (`LIFECYCLE_EVENTS`) including `log.suppressed` itself, since shedding the record that reports shedding makes every other loss unattributable; anomaly is `warn|error|fatal` outside that set, which is what separates a `diagnostic` legacy-failure line from routine `diagnostic` narration; everything else is sample. **`deliveryStage` markers are sample** and stated so in source: they carry a stage rather than an event so the table cannot classify them, they are the highest-volume producer on this descriptor at one marker per frame per hop, and their value is statistical rather than per-record. **The hard constraint, deliberately not waived:** lifecycle got a high ceiling, NOT no ceiling — an unconditional enqueue turns a wedged reader into an unbounded leak, i.e. the exact failure the non-blocking queue exists to prevent. 1MB holds ~3,000 lifecycle records, orders of magnitude past what a real session emits, and a lifecycle record shed at that ceiling still goes through `noteDrop` so the histogram stays exact. **Verification:** 2 new tests (three-class shedding ladder; lifecycle-ceiling-vs-sample-ceiling comparison) + 2 CC-41 tests updated to the new behavior, **all 4 confirmed failing against pre-change sources** in a detached worktree at `df393287` and passing after; focused logger suite **8/0**; app typecheck clean; sidecar wrapper passed (5,567 upstream ignored); hardening **19/19**; `bun test app/` **3042/10**, all 10 the same `*.probe.test.ts` live-engine suites CC-38/CC-41 recorded, failing on `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY or CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN env var is required`. No protocol, preload, inbound-frame, or locked-decision change. **Carry-forward:** `app/main/operationalLogSink.ts` sheds independently under `rate_dedupe` and has no priority classes; unflagged because it is a different mechanism, not because it is known-fine. |

Both alternatives are foreclosed by the record contract, not merely awkward: `sanitizeOperationalFields` (`operationalLog.ts:178-198`) throws on arrays and objects and caps at 16 fields, and a burst of twelve backdated `renderer.health.sample` records would be collapsed by the main sink's 1000ms `${event}:${appSessionId}:${reason}` dedup (`operationalLogSink.ts:115-123`) into one plus eleven `rate_dedupe` suppressions, destroying the evidence this exists to preserve. Whole readings are dropped newest-kept-first when the budget runs out, because `sanitizeOperationalText` truncates mid-string and would corrupt an entry rather than lose one. `flush()` clears the ring, so a crash that trips both triggers writes the evidence once. **Vocabulary is additive:** the new `renderer.health.flight_recorder` event, its `['reason','count','samples']` entry in `OPERATIONAL_EVENT_FIELD_KEYS` (without it the `?? []` fallback at `:190` forbids EVERY field, which is why `window.created` still logs none), and `samples` added to the key universe. `diagnosticsBundle.ts` needed no change: it admits records through `parseOperationalRecord`, which validates against the same list, so the new event exports for free. **Verification:** `mainDecisions.test.ts` **57/0** with 4 new tests; fail-before proven in a detached HEAD worktree (all new tests fail on the missing export; with the ring present but the vocabulary absent, exactly the record-contract test fails, first on the unknown event and then on `unsafe field reason` once only the event name was added, pinning both halves of the vocabulary change), plus three mutations each failing exactly one test (no byte budget, no ring clear, oldest-kept-instead-of-newest). `bun test app/` **3035/10** — all 10 are the `*.probe.test.ts` live-engine suites failing on `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY or CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN env var is required`, the same baseline CC-38/CC-41/CC-42 recorded. `app typecheck` clean; sidecar wrapper passed (5,567 upstream ignored); hardening **19/19**. No protocol, preload, inbound-frame, renderer, or locked-decision change; no user-visible surface. **§0 flag, carry-forward:** main's three wiring points (record on response, flush on gone, flush on unavailable) are in `main.ts`, which imports Electron and so has no unit-test layer; they are verified by inspection only, exactly the split `mainDecisions.ts` exists to formalize. A live crash writing a real `renderer.health.flight_recorder` record remains unobserved. |

| **CC-40 · Reserve inbound rate capacity for user actions; review fixes on CC-38** → `app/shared/limits.ts`, `app/preload/{rendererIpcGuard,deliveryAckQueue,preload}.ts`, `app/main/main.ts`, `app/sidecar/operationalLogger.ts`, `docs/migration/decisions/IPC-RATE-BUDGET.md` | ✅ **CODE+HEADLESS DONE 2026-08-09** · ⬜ **operator GUI acceptance PENDING** | **Completes the CC-38 incident.** New `IPC-RATE-BUDGET.md` decision: a sub-cap INSIDE the existing total, not a second budget beside it. `MAX_FRAMES_PER_WINDOW` stays 120 so T7's flood surface does not move; `MAX_DIAGNOSTIC_FRAMES_PER_WINDOW = 80` bounds the diagnostics class, leaving at least 40 slots always available to control traffic. Control is deliberately not sub-capped. The acknowledgement flush is the only diagnostics-class sender; the fault reporter (already bounded to 12/min, and most valuable exactly when the window saturates) and the health response (a false miss would manufacture a fake outage) stay control, per §4. **Review fixes on `0cbc19ad`** (two agents, both verified against source): the ack flush now RETAINS and retries a rejected batch instead of dropping it, because `updateWatermarks` advances by a contiguous scan, so one discarded batch pinned `applied` below `produced` for the rest of the stream and `firstMissing` then reported a renderer stall that never happened, which is the very symptom this incident was chasing; retained acks are bounded at 1024, dropping newest so the contiguous scan can still resume; the scheduling boolean became a timer handle, so the flag no longer lies and surplus no-op timers stop being armed per 64 acks; the health-probe response is wrapped like the other two telemetry senders; the T7 source test now pins that `ipcRenderer.send` sits INSIDE the try AFTER the guard, which the original assertion did not. Also dev-only stderr for `did-fail-load` / `render-process-gone` (scoped honestly: neither fired during this incident). **Report amended** with a second symptom the review found: `App.tsx:875` acknowledges before projection, so a pre-fix rejection aborted the subscribe callback and the whole frame batch never reached any store, meaning real frame loss, not only lost evidence. **Second review round (2026-08-09, two more agents) found and fixed:** nothing pinned the `'diagnostics'` argument, so deleting one token silently restored the starvation with a fully green battery; the sub-cap's window reset was likewise untested, and deleting it would have throttled diagnostics for the life of the process; the health probe was wrapped but never added to the source test that exists to pin wrapping; the retry treated EVERY guard rejection as transient, so a size or serialization rejection would have spun on identical bytes forever (unreachable at current constants, 26.7 KB against a 128 KiB limit, but the guard now reports a typed reason and only a rate rejection retries); `render-process-gone` fires with `clean-exit` on an ordinary quit and `did-fail-load` with `-3` on ordinary dev reloads, so both stderr lines are filtered where the operational record is not. Both new pins verified failing without their fix. **Doc corrections:** §5 of the decision record contradicted §4 by implying control-class senders must throw, which would have mandated the CC-38 bug; budget class and failure class are now stated as separate axes. The window is fixed, not sliding, so the reservation is per window and a straddling interval can carry 240 total. **Verification:** focused renderer/preload/supervisor/sidecar-logger **1958/0** then preload **15/0** after the added pins; app typecheck clean; sidecar wrapper passed (5,561 upstream ignored); hardening **19/19**; renderer build passed. The two strengthened T7 source assertions are regression guards and pass against the prior source; the fail-before evidence belongs to CC-38's boundary tests, not to this row. **Open item closed 2026-08-09:** Extracted delivery acknowledgement queue into Electron-free `deliveryAckQueue.ts` with 16 behavioural unit tests (`deliveryAckQueue.test.ts`) covering rate retry, size/serialize drop, single timer, bounded trimming (drop newest), retrying clear, order preservation, push-time epoch stamping, guard-before-send, and diagnostics budget class. `preload.ts` delegates to it; `preloadSource.test.ts` updated to check both files. Battery green: 30/30 preload tests, typecheck clean, sidecar typecheck clean, hardening 19/19. The 80 sub-cap is arithmetic, not measurement: guarded sends per window per class are still unrecorded. **⚖ Two-strikes waive (both review rounds flagged it, so it is recorded rather than dismissed a third time):** the early `return` in the acknowledgement flush was twice reported as stranding the queue remainder with no timer armed. WAIVED as unreachable, not fixed: every push checks `length >= MAX_DELIVERY_ACKS_PER_BATCH` and the flush splices the whole array, so at flush entry the remainder is always empty. The retain-and-retry change made a remainder real, and that path re-arms explicitly, which is why the finding is closed rather than open. If the batch-threshold check on the push path is ever removed or reordered, this waive expires and the finding is live again. **⬜ Follow-up (CC-40, extraction):** the queue now lives in an Electron-free `app/preload/deliveryAckQueue.ts` with behavioural tests (`d6637e3e`); the epoch-at-push invariant was mutation-verified independently, and one doc comment claiming push never sends inline was corrected (`adb2c387`). |
| **CC-39 · Desktop diagnostics answer renderer-loss and recording-coverage questions directly** → `app/shared/{operationalLog,deliveryTrace}.ts`, `app/main/{main,mainDecisions,deliveryTraceSink,diagnosticsBundle}.ts`, `app/supervisor/supervisor.ts` | 🟡 **CODE LANDED 2026-08-09** (`c0ec60e4`, `8d463da8`) · hardening green · aggregate probe flake | **Consumer-driven follow-up to CC-38.** Sustained renderer health loss now escalates from one `renderer.health.missed` warning at ~20 s to recurring error-level `renderer.health.unavailable` evidence from ~35 s onward, refreshed every 60 s with the current miss count and elapsed time since the last response; recovery still closes the episode with duration. `renderer.unresponsive` stays warn because Electron can emit it for a transient event-loop stall without proving UI loss; `renderer.process.gone` stays error because process death is definitive, and the new sustained-health event now covers the error-level middle. A main-owned `log.coverage.incomplete` is written on every sidecar exit because the bounded sidecar diagnostics queue has no flush acknowledgement; clean exits are conservatively marked incomplete rather than falsely claiming complete evidence. Export adds one `recordingCoverage` answer with active/completed/interrupted launches, known suppression/trace-loss counts, and incomplete-stream count, so a reader need not infer recording continuity across records. It also marks coverage incomplete when a retained source exceeds the export tail window, the bundle admission cap is reached, or a source read fails, so bounded export cannot hide older loss evidence behind a false complete status. Delivery records add closed `observationKind: action|acknowledgement`; anomalies add closed `anomalyScope: source_sequence|stage_sequence`, while the second-pass export parser remains backward-compatible with older retained records and rejects mismatched new fields. Dev startup prints the private diagnostics directory once. **Incident premise checked:** retained local logs contain the expected `renderer.health.missed` records at ~20 s and recoveries spanning 10–105 s, so no second missing-detector defect was established; exact identity with the consumer's copy is unprovable. **Privacy/security:** metadata-only closed fields; no payload, prompt, transcript, credential, inbound frame, preload channel, protocol, or locked-decision change. **Diagnostic fidelity vs privacy:** privacy wins for the legacy `diagnostic` bridge; it remains only a `legacy_failure|legacy_notice` look-elsewhere hint and is explicitly **not counted as diagnostic coverage**. **Verification:** focused diagnostics/supervisor **80/0**; app typecheck clean; sidecar wrapper passed (5,561 upstream ignored); renderer build passed; hardening **19/19**. Committed `migration` clone at `8f527f97` contained both diagnostics commits. The hardening-owned sidecar was correlated to `/tmp/catcode-37792/s0.sock` and terminated by exact PID; a pre-existing sidecar was not touched. **⬜ Cuts/residuals:** delivery tracing still runs at the measured default volume and its main-owned synchronous writer can contend with the event path on slow storage; changing sampling/default or moving the writer needs a separately measured design. No closed legacy-diagnostic vocabulary was added. |
| **CC-38 · Renderer unmounted to a black window when delivery acknowledgements exhausted the IPC rate cap** → `app/preload/preload.ts`, `app/renderer/src/{RendererErrorBoundary,main}.tsx`, `app/sidecar/{operationalLogger,index}.ts`, `docs/reports/2026-08-09-renderer-black-window-delivery-ack-rate-cap.md` | ✅ **CODE+HEADLESS DONE 2026-08-09** · ⬜ **operator GUI acceptance PENDING** | **Live incident, diagnosed from the operator's console + desktop logs.** Delivery-trace acknowledgements ride the same shared inbound budget (`MAX_FRAMES_PER_WINDOW` 120/1000ms) as real user input. `queueMicrotask` coalesced nothing in steady state (each frame arrives in its own task), so under a burst the effect at `App.tsx:603` crossed the 64-item branch and flushed inline, and the guard's rejection surfaced on a React effect stack. The boundary caught it, then reported the fault through the SAME spent budget, so `componentDidCatch` threw: React discards the fallback and unmounts the tree. Fixes: the fault reporter and the ack flush now contain the rejection (the guard still runs, so an over-budget payload is DROPPED rather than sent, T7 unchanged), the flush batches on a 50ms timer, and the boundary plus both global handlers no longer depend on the bridge existing. Also stamps `appSessionId` on every sidecar operational record: `legacy` and `log.suppressed` omitted it, so `supervisor.ts:253` discarded both — including the record that reports dropping, which is why the trace could not distinguish lost frames from lost trace records. **Verification:** focused renderer/preload/supervisor/sidecar-logger suites **1955/0**; both new boundary tests confirmed failing before the fix; app typecheck clean; sidecar wrapper passed (5,561 upstream ignored); hardening **19/19**; renderer build passed. `bun test app/` 2984/10 — all 10 are `*.probe.test.ts` live suites failing on `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY or CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN env var is required`, unrelated to this change. **⬜ Operator GUI:** run a session that streams a long turn and confirm the window no longer blanks; if a renderer error does occur, the recovery panel should appear instead of black. **Open:** reserving budget so telemetry cannot starve user input is NOT fixed here; it moves a T7 boundary and needs a decision record. |

The driver now asks the host's existing read-time `canResume` predicate before selecting every TTL or cap victim. This deliberately rejects both a never-materialized transcript and a transcript deleted after a completed turn; `lastMessageSentAt === null` was considered but rejected as only a partial signal, since a later deletion retains a send timestamp. The predicate also respects the host's in-memory `resumeFailed` verdict. | **CC-36 · Four surfaces still called an intentional park a crash** → `app/shared/hostApi.ts`, `app/host/host.ts`, `app/renderer/src/{sessionStatusVisual,tabStatus,SessionsPage,commandPaletteModel,sidebarState,debugStateReport,sessionsCatalogState}.ts`, `docs/migration/decisions/IDLE-PARK.md` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-08-09** (local `migration`, `396bebc`) · headless green · ⬜ **operator GUI acceptance PENDING** | **Closes IDLE-PARK §1b**, open since 2026-08-05. A parked session's descriptor WAS byte-identical to a crash by design (`disconnected` + `restorable`, so `foldTabMembership` keeps the tab); it now differs by exactly one bit, and that status pairing is unchanged. §1a fixed the three chat-view surfaces that read the renderer's own connection snapshot, but the Sessions page `StatusBadge`, the ⌘K palette (visible chip AND its `crashed, restorable` aria-label), the sidebar row's aria-label and the debug export are **descriptor-derived** and never see that snapshot, so they went on calling an intentional reclaim `crashed`. The debug export — the GUI-VERIFICATION cross-check source — disagreed with itself, `sidebar[]` saying `crashed` while `tabs[]` said `idle` for the same session. **Operator chose option (B):** `SessionDescriptor` gains `parked`, set in `host.descriptorFromRow` where the row's shutdown marking is already computed; all four inherit it through the one shared `sessionStatusVisual` vocabulary, which now returns the same `{tone:'busy', label:'idle'}` the tab already used. **This reverses §11**, which rejected exactly this field — its stated reason was keeping the renderer provably unaware park exists, and the 2026-08-05 ruling withdrew that goal, so the premise no longer held. Descriptor otherwise unchanged. **Two gates kept, both mutation-pinned:** `restorable` (§1c — a session parked before its first turn has no transcript, `canResume` refuses it, so it stays honestly dead rather than dressed up as resting) and `liveStatus === null` (an unpark spawns BEFORE `upsertOnSpawn` clears the row's mark, so reading the mark alone would paint a booting engine as resting and swallow `starting`). **`tabStatus.ts`'s own parked branch was folded back behind the shared module** — it was a fifth label+tone decision living outside the module that exists to stop exactly that drift, which is why the four surfaces could not inherit it. **New `parkedSurfaces.test.ts` checks all four TOGETHER**, because each already had its own suite and each passed while collectively getting this wrong; the defect was that nothing asked whether they AGREE. `parked` is required, not optional, so every descriptor construction site had to answer (37 fixtures updated). **Battery:** `bun test app/` **2913 pass / 0 fail** (198 files) · `app typecheck` clean · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `renderer:build` ok. Sidecar typecheck red only on the untracked `app/shared/deliveryTrace.ts` (another session's file, unchanged). **Reviewed 2026-08-09 (RED verdict, 9 findings, all valid) and closed in `8a91484` + `e245251`.** Eight fixed. **One fixed differently from how it was written up:** the review asked for the close path's completeness rule on the startup-backfill writer too; measuring real caches showed that would be wrong, because a backfilled cache's frames come from `toSDKMessages` and carry ZERO `result` frames and zero frame-level `permissionMode`, so its fallback names the model and nothing else and a partial header is strictly better than none. Both writers now share the honest floor (never stamp an all-null header) and keep the rule their own frames justify. The rest: an authoritative `run_facts` snapshot's `effort: null` means provider-default and is no longer patched from a cached value; close-time token extraction now reproduces `contextUsage.ts`'s compaction rules (floor at the boundary, skip the preserved originals spliced above it — a plain newest-wins scan lands squarely on them); carry-forward is bound to the engine session id; `IDLE-PARK.md` contradicted itself in six places after the §1b reversal and is reconciled; the aggregate four-surface test asserted the shared vocabulary as a PROXY for the Sessions page and never touched the debug export, and now renders the real page and builds the real snapshot with both production arguments mutation-verified; the account-dot test used an `isDefault:false` account production can never select as active. **`e245251` closes the last one:** the retained rail snapshots had no session-removal cleanup, so full model option lists stayed keyed by removed ids until renderer restart. **⬜ ACCEPTED WITH BOUND, not fixed — F4:** the close/park/quit transcript scan is synchronous on Electron main. It was UNBOUNDED (a large enough transcript could stall or exhaust the process) and is now capped at 64 MB before the read. The reviewer's fuller disposition — move the scan off main's synchronous path — is **declined on measurement**: 17 ms for the largest transcript on this machine (13 MB), ~85 ms at the cap, against a real risk of losing a cache, because the documented **snapshot → persist → evict** ordering requires the write to complete before the frames are dropped and an async read breaks it. **Re-open only on evidence:** a visible hitch when a session closes or parks, which needs a transcript several times larger than any that exists here. **⬜ Still open on park:** §1c (a session with no transcript should never be parked at all — needs the host's `canResume` exposed to the park path) and §1d (park is now visually indistinguishable from live, and the disarmed run-control pickers give no click feedback — three options written up, operator ruling needed). |

Delivery worked and no text was lost, but the row exposed reclamation machinery that `IDLE-PARK.md` §1a requires the chat view to hide. **Root cause:** `PendingSubmitState` retained only the text. The submit decision knew this was a park (`planSessionSubmit` saw `connectionStatus:'parked'`; the drain's `resolvePendingSubmit` returned `'restore'`), but after `restoreSession` advanced the connection to `connecting`, `SessionPane` saw the same shape as a genuine first cold spawn and rendered CC-16's load-bearing row. **Fix:** a pending submit now retains `{text, showQueuedRow}` from the original submit decision. Cold spawn/preview waits stamp `true` and preserve the existing row unchanged; park restore stamps `false` and keeps it through `parked → connecting → ready`. The same record still disables a second hold, drains exactly once via the existing restore/`app.submit` path, and `releasePendingSubmit` still restores `pending.text` with the existing `Your message was not sent...` message on refusal, throw or terminal failure. No replacement UI was invented. **Boundary:** renderer-local only; no protocol, preload, sidecar, inbound vocabulary or security-baseline change. `IDLE-PARK.md` §1a now records this fifth chat-view surface. **Tests:** focused composer/submit path **152 pass / 0 fail**; mutation forcing the park flag visible failed at the new assertion, then the restored implementation returned to 152/0. Stale-reference sweep covered code, tests, docs and config; historical rows/reports remain frozen, and the parity-ledger row stays accurate because the real-added queue still exists for cold spawn. **§0:** no new visual element; genuine cold-spawn behavior unchanged, park-only machinery narration removed. **Unchanged/out of scope:** §1b descriptor-derived crash readings and §1d disarmed-picker click feedback. **⬜ Operator GUI:** force a real idle park with five sessions, submit in the parked target, confirm no queue row flashes and the normal user turn appears after restore; separately create a genuine cold spawn and confirm the existing queue row still appears. **Cleanup note:** hardening left PID `87139` on its own `/tmp/catcode-87102/s0.sock`; exact-PID termination was denied twice by the configured safety hook after ownership correlation, so it remains reported rather than bypassed. |
| **CC-34 · A blocked subagent told the user "Needs you" for a handoff that goes to the assistant** → `app/renderer/src/orchestratorState.ts`, `app/renderer/src/agentIdentity.ts`, `app/renderer/src/AgentChrome.tsx`, `app/renderer/src/TasksDialog.tsx`, `app/renderer/src/OrchestratorRoster.tsx`, `app/renderer/src/App.tsx`, `src/components/PromptInput/Notifications.tsx` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-08-09** (local `migration`) · headless green · ⬜ **operator GUI acceptance PENDING** | **Operator report:** *"application have ui when subagent need me. it show as 'need you'. I dont think the agent need me. what it need is a model input which isnt 'me'."* Correct, and the drift was already on record: `OrchestratorRoster.tsx:23-30` had flagged this exact mechanism "for a ruling rather than resolved here" since P4-32a. **Root cause — the escalation asked the wrong question.** A blocked subagent's handoff (`handoffStatus:'blocked'`, parsed from its own result text at `src/tasks/LocalAgentTask/LocalAgentTask.tsx:501,520`) is enqueued as a `task-notification` to the PARENT conversation (`:273-344` → `src/utils/messageQueueManager.ts:179`) and drained into a fresh turn with no human action — desktop `app/sidecar/sidecarServer.ts:1209` `drainOneTaskNotification` → `startTurn`, terminal `src/hooks/useQueueProcessor.ts:48-60`; a foreground subagent's blocker is simply the `tool_result`. The renderer nevertheless escalated on `AgentModeSnapshot.active`, which is the engine's `isAgentMode()` (`src/agent-mode/agentMode.ts:39`, env `CLAUDE_CODE_AGENT_MODE`) — that answers *which persona the parent runs*, not *whether a parent exists*. It is off in every ordinary desktop session, so **the false alarm was the default, not the exception**. A second surface was worse: `deriveTaskAgentState` (`agentIdentity.ts`, behind the /tasks Tasks tab) returned `'needs-you'` for a blocked `local_agent` with no orchestrator axis at all, so enabling agent mode could not correct it. **Fix:** blocked → `'waiting'` and owner → assistant, unconditionally, on both derivations. The only other evictor, `getUnifiedTaskAttachments` (`src/utils/attachments.ts:3456`), runs at turn START, which is by construction too early: the 30s grace has not elapsed on the completion-notification turn that immediately follows, and an idle session starts no further turn. It is in fact fully reachable on the desktop, traced end to end: `QueryEngine.ts:462` → `processUserInput.ts:497` (no `skipAttachments`) → `attachments.ts:755`, where the session sidecar sets neither `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE` nor `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ATTACHMENTS` (only the auxiliary workers do) and `isMainThread` is true because `QueryEngine` sets no `agentId`; it writes through `toolUseContext.setAppState`, the SAME store this reaper uses (`sessionController.ts:385-386` vs `:626`). A direct probe confirms that path evicts exactly this task shape. This reaper adds a trigger, so it cannot succeed where the turn path already failed. **What it DOES close, and why it stays:** a session that goes idle immediately after a worker finishes reaches no turn boundary at all, which is the terminal panel tick's whole reason for existing; the desktop had no equivalent owner. **Guards ruled out for this session:** blocked handoff (it stamps no `evictAfter`, but `orchestratorWorkerState` renders blocked as purple `waiting` / amber `needs-you`, and the operator screenshot shows the green `completed` pip); persisted-plane re-add (no state file exists). **Unresolved:** which guard actually refused. Infinity) > Date.now()` guard refuses forever and the reaper's deadline scan skips undefined deadlines (`panelTaskReaper.ts` `earliestDeadline`). That row renders purple `waiting` (not the reported green; the operator's row was @Wilkes/@Feynman) and its persistence is intended engine semantics — the TUI keeps blocked rows too (`CoordinatorAgentStatus.tsx:32`) but offers dismiss (x → `stopOrDismissAgent` → `evictAfter: 0`, `PromptInput.tsx:1872`), which the desktop lacked until the follow-up below shipped the same escape hatch. **Follow-up, RESOLVED 2026-08-09 (dismiss verb landed, local `migration`):** the operator chose the desktop dismiss verb over an engine-side deadline for a consumed blocked handoff, so the engine's blocked-rows-persist semantics are untouched and the terminal REPL is unaffected. `task.dismiss` joins the EXISTING `TASK_CONTROL_VERB_TYPES` family (additive; no protocol version bump): sidecar-local Zod discriminated-union schema, `checkStrictKeys` entry allowing only `{type,requestId,taskId}` so no forged `evictAfter`/`retain` can reach the engine's guards, five boundary tests (valid dispatch routed to dismiss not stop, non-string taskId, missing requestId, forged `evictAfter` key, absent domain) plus a live-path test over a real blocked-worker store. 'current'` (`src/agent-mode/sessionState.ts:672`), so a same-session PERSISTED worker (one whose live task the reaper already evicted) renders as a current-looking row that offered Dismiss, and the verb then refused it `not_found` while the row stayed put with a toast contradicting the screen. Fixed by making `not_found` a retirement rather than a refusal: it means nothing LIVE holds the row, leaving the persisted twin as the only thing rendering it, which is exactly what the suppression retires. `still_running` / `unsupported_type` stay refusals, because there the engine holds real state and hiding the row would be a display lie. This needed the refusal CODE to survive the domain boundary (it had been flattened into prose), and the two-plane composition now lives in `sidecarServer.dismissWorker` where both planes are visible. Second fix: the dismissal mark was permanent, so a worker RESUMED under the same agentId would have its persisted twin suppressed forever once the resumed run ended; `dismissedStillPending` now drops a mark the moment its worker is live again, which also stops the set outliving what it describes. Also pinned the reaper hand-off that three doc comments rest on (a blocked worker gets no timer at all, gains one from the `evictAfter: 0` mark, and is swept once `notified` clears) and corrected a wrong comment: the explicit re-broadcast is required because the `not_found` path mutates no store and fires no subscription, NOT because the subscription frames raced. Reviewer-raised and rejected: broadcast-ordering (all three snapshots read the dismissed set after their own await, and microtasks precede fs callbacks, so they are identical). **CLOSED 2026-08-09 (CC-32 follow-up):** broadcastAgentModeSnapshot now reads the state file once per broadcast and fans the resulting payload to every connection; its attach path still fetches once for the newly attached connection. The reaper's 1s retry deliberately has no bounded give-up: it only remains armed for a terminal task past its deadline when the engine still refuses eviction because completion is not yet notified or its notification is queued. Giving up would strand that row above the composer forever, recreating the symptom this reaper exists to fix; the retry costs one timer and a scan of the small task set, with no I/O. If notified never flips, the failed completion delivery is the defect to diagnose, while continuing to retry is the correct visible-state behavior. **Not closed:** operator GUI acceptance of both the reaper and this Dismiss control. **Review pass (`/review-impl`, 2 agents):** found a HIGH defect I had introduced — the past-due retry re-armed a fresh relative 1s on EVERY app-state change, so sub-second store churn during a live turn postponed the sweep indefinitely, reproducing the very symptom. No preview spawns an engine and the heavyweight worker is not relaunched. **The correctness constraint that shaped it:** the renderer trusts a `runFacts` header WHOLESALE and stops consulting frames (`selectPreviewRunFacts`), so a thin header is worse than none — an on-close cache's frames still yield model, permission mode, usage and the exact window off a live `result`. `resolveCacheRunFacts` therefore writes a header ONLY when complete on all four; `effort` alone may be null, which is correct for provider-default sessions. Both regression tests fail on the pre-fix behaviour (proved by neutering the gate) and pass after. **The sibling leak the operator reported in the same breath is now CC-33**, not an open item on this row. |

(1) The account switcher was ARMED during `connecting`/`starting`, because `connectionHasEngine` is true there and `provider` now survives the lifecycle null; the verb is fire-and-forget, so the supervisor's `session_not_ready` surfaced nowhere and the pane tracked a requestId that never resolved. It is replaced by `composerRailModel.ts`, a real derivation with a real test, and all five behaviours are now mutation-verified (each fix was broken, the test went red, the break was reverted). Three behaviours that had NO test (the null-model borrow, the re-attach mode carry, accounts freshness) gained one. **Battery after the fix pass:** every suite covering owned files **149 pass / 0 fail**; `test:hardening` **19/19**; `renderer:build` ok. **Not mine, unfixed, and currently RED:** `bun test app/` reports 11 failures and `app typecheck` reports errors, ALL in the agent-mode/attention cluster another session is mid-refactor on (`orchestratorState.ts` and `agentIdentity.ts` are dirty with an `attention` removal; the failures read `Expected: "needs-you" / Received: "waiting"`), plus `app/shared/deliveryTrace.ts` (untracked, TS2740) which also fails `typecheck:sidecar`. Both batteries were green on this work before those edits landed mid-run. **Operator GUI acceptance 2026-08-09 — PASSED with three findings, two fixed in `6140eea`.** Driving a real park found faces the headless tests could not: **(1)** the fast ⚡ vanished when fast mode was OFF, because the read-only face rendered only the ON state — correct while its only caller was a preview (a cache says nothing about fast, so "off" and "unknown" were one thing) and wrong once a detached LIVE session could answer. `fastMode` is now tri-state, and a model that cannot do fast reports null so no face renders, matching the live chip's own `supportedByModel` behaviour. **(2)** The account status **dot** was dropped: the alias text is deliberately never health-tinted, so the dot is the only thing carrying which account this is and how it is doing, and a detached pane cannot open the switcher to go looking for it. Both mutation-verified. **(3) ⬜ NOT FIXED, handed to a next session:** submitting on a parked session flashes the CC-16 `Queued — Sends when the session is ready.` row for about a second before the restore lands, so the restore narrates itself. That row is load-bearing for a genuine cold spawn and must not simply be deleted; the renderer already separates the two cases (`resolvePendingSubmit` returns `restore` for parked, `wait` for spawning). It is the last chat-view surface still announcing a park. **Battery after the GUI pass:** `bun test app/` **2907 pass / 0 fail** · `app typecheck` clean · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `renderer:build` ok. (The agent-mode/attention failures noted above cleared when that session finished; only the untracked `app/shared/deliveryTrace.ts` TS2740 remains, still not mine.) **⬜ Related, still open:** IDLE-PARK §1b's four descriptor-derived surfaces (Sessions page, ⌘K palette, sidebar aria-label, debug export) still read a park as `crashed`; and the NEW §1d, which this row created — park is now visually indistinguishable from a live session and the disarmed pickers give no feedback on click. Three options are written up there; it needs an operator ruling, not a patch. |
| **CC-30 · The Leases tab was unreadable: raw account UUIDs, engine log prose, and three disagreeing counts** → `app/renderer/src/leaseState.ts`, `app/renderer/src/TasksDialog.tsx` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-08-08** (local `migration`) · headless green · ⬜ **operator GUI acceptance NOT PASSED 2026-08-08, then repaired** — the operator rejected it on sight: the panel showed `Main thread 0s` on an account the main thread was never using. **Root cause:** with the main thread on the Anthropic path no Codex lease is registered at all (`src/query.ts:324` gates on `getAPIProvider() === 'openai'`), so `getCodexLeaseSnapshot` fell back to `synthesizeMainLease`, which reports the pool's ACTIVE account and re-mints `createdAt` on EVERY snapshot. The row therefore asserted an account the main thread was not consuming, its held duration could never read anything but `0s`, and it inflated both the tab count and the `All N agents` note. **A review agent flagged the `0s` and it was dismissed as "predates this diff" — wrong call: the surface asserting it was new.** Fixed by marking the synthesised lease `selectionKind: 'synthetic'` engine-side and DROPPING it at the sidecar; the engine's kind union is now a superset of the wire union, and a `ProjectableLease` type guard makes that drop a compile-time property rather than a convention. Operator also called the mono treatment bland, so chroma is back on the account name and the strategy value (teal), body rows still neutral. ⬜ **re-acceptance PENDING** | **Operator report:** *"everything is unreadable. Too many technical/bad format and many problems."* Confirmed against source, with three defects behind it. **(1)** the panel printed the engine's `selectionReason` verbatim, which embeds the same 36-char account UUID twice (`` `failover from <uuid>: Codex account <uuid> …` ``, `codexAccountLeaseManager.ts:333`) and otherwise reads as internal log prose (`spread selected least crowded healthy account`, `:551`). **(2)** the tab count, the `N leases` chip and the `Owners · N` heading printed **three different numbers** for one session, because the engine's per-account rollup omits a synthesised main lease (`:168-170,438`) while the owner list includes it. **(3)** a failed lease keeps the account id it could not use (`:356-362`) and still displayed its previous SUCCESS reason, so a stranded agent read as healthy. **Rebuilt grouped by account** (`selectLeaseGroups`): the account heads the group, agents nest under a rail that brackets heading and rows together, and a stranded bucket leads the list. Agents now lead with their real minted handle (`selectWorkerDisplayName` over `AgentModeWorkerItem.handle`, joined `ownerId === agentId`) instead of the delegated task text. All ids and engine prose are replaced by humanised notes; account ids are stripped from the hover detail too, since CLAUDE.md §7 counts a `title` as a text surface. One count everywhere (`selectLeaseAgentCount` over `owners`). **Operator vocabulary ruling:** the word "lease" is engine vocabulary and now appears nowhere a user can read it — the tab is **Accounts**, asserted by test. **Deleted as dead:** `LEASE_STATE_META` + `selectActiveLeaseCount`; `state:'released'` is unreachable because `releaseCodexLease` DELETES the map entry (`:294-296`), so only `active` and `failed` ever cross the seam. **Not fixed (engine-side, needs an owner):** the account an agent moved *off* cannot be named, since `LeaseSnapshot.accounts` lists only accounts currently holding one (`protocol.ts:1343`) — naming it needs the alias projected at the sidecar. **Self-review (`/review-impl`) caught five things the battery could not**, all fixed in the same pass: an un-aliased account still headed its group with a raw 36-char id (`accountAlias` is nullable and every test fixture happened to set one, so the id-leak test could not see it); the concentration note claimed "All N agents" while the tab chip showed a larger N whenever an agent was stranded; the main row took its name from the engine's `ownerLabel`, which the synthesised path spells lowercase (`codexAccountLeaseManager.ts:432`), so it read "main thread main"; raw UUIDs survived in the `title` tooltip; and deleting `LEASE_STATE_META` removed the renderer's only compile-time tripwire on the `LeaseState` union, restored as `LEASE_STATE_ROLE`. **Also:** the first commit (`a4d4249`) shipped `leaseState.ts` containing a literal NUL byte in a map-key constant, which made git treat the file as binary and would have made `rg` skip it during traversal; removed. **Both follow-ups then closed in the same session (`f00f572`)** rather than deferred: they shared a root cause, in that the engine already knew why a lease moved and which account it left, then discarded both into an interpolated sentence. `CodexLease` now carries `selectionKind` (`initial|failover|repaired|manual`, required, so a new construction site cannot forget it) and `previousAccountId`; the protocol gains `LeaseSelectionKind` + `LeaseMovedFrom` additively (no version bump, same disclosure policy as the existing `accountId`/`accountAlias` pair); the SIDECAR resolves the source account to its alias, which only it can do because `accountAliases()` reads the whole pool while the snapshot rollup carries only accounts currently holding a lease, and omits the field entirely when the pool no longer knows that account so a bare id never crosses. The renderer branches on the kind instead of prefix-matching prose and now names the account: `moved here from aurora`. **Not mine, unfixed, no owner:** `codexAccountLeaseManager.test.ts` "terminal cap failure reports quota exhaustion from the typed decision" fails with `account_recovery` where it expects `quota_exhausted`. Verified pre-existing by re-running it at the pre-change baseline, where it fails identically; it asserts `withRetry` terminal-failure classification, which this work never touches. Design explored with the operator across three HTML option sets under `docs/design-html/2026-08-08-accounts-*.html` (layout chosen: hairline rail; colour: mono, chroma reserved for the stranded state). |
| **CC-29 · A message typed mid-response waited for the response instead of reaching it** → `app/sidecar/sidecarServer.ts`, `app/renderer/src/composerState.ts`, `app/renderer/src/App.tsx` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-08-06** (local `migration`) · headless green · ⬜ **operator GUI acceptance PENDING** | **Operator report:** the queued row's `Sends when this response finishes.` — *"I think we found a wrong behavior in the app. In cat-code tui, i dont think it 'send when response finished'."* **Correct, and the label was accurate about the app, so the BEHAVIOR was the defect.** **Root cause — CC-26 cited the fallback path as if it were the rule.** Its row anchors the terminal's mid-turn typing on `useCommandQueue` → `executeQueuedInput` (`src/screens/REPL.tsx:652,4428-4430`), i.e. `useQueueProcessor`, which runs only when NO query is active (`src/hooks/useQueueProcessor.ts:48-51`). That is the terminal's fallback. The rule is that the RUNNING query loop drains the same queue at every tool round and injects what it finds into the turn already in flight: `getCommandsByMaxPriority('next')` (`src/query.ts:1636-1645`) → `getQueuedCommandAttachments`, where `mode:'prompt'` is an inline mode (`src/utils/attachments.ts:1056`); query.ts states it outright at `:1605-1606` (*"sent as attachments so Claude can respond to them in the current turn"*). So in the terminal a mid-response message reaches the model DURING the response; the desktop parked it and started a fresh turn afterwards. **Fix — put the prompt where the engine's own drain will find it.** The sidecar's `handleSubmit` no longer answers `turn_already_running` on a live turn: it calls `enqueueMidTurnPrompt`, which broadcasts the user message and `enqueue`s `{mode:'prompt', agentId:undefined}` (default `'next'` priority — the exact filter that drain applies). The queue is process-global and this process owns one engine session (N-process model), so there is no cross-session leak. Renderer-side, `planSessionSubmit` returns `send` on `turnPending` and `resolvePendingSubmit` returns `send` for any `ready` connection regardless of `inputEnabled`; the park now serves ONLY its original purpose, a spawn with no engine attached yet. **No new frame kind, preload channel, inbound vocabulary, protocol field or error code — `app.submit` is the same frame, validated by the same T4/T7/trust gates in the same order** (the trust gate still precedes the queue, so nothing reaches the engine at an untrusted cwd). **Fallback half:** a turn that ends with no further tool round never runs the engine's drain, so `drainOneQueuedPrompt` starts a turn for the leftover at the sidecar boundary — the terminal's `useQueueProcessor` edge — with `announcePrompt:false` so the message is never printed twice, and it is tried BEFORE a queued worker result, mirroring the engine's `'next'`-over-`'later'` order. The idle-park gate gained the matching check (`hasQueuedParentPrompt`), and the awaiting-durable-acceptance latch moved off the SCHEDULER onto the task-notification drain, since a human prompt is the very thing that clears it. **Closes a CC-26 ⬜ owed item as a side effect:** the fire-and-forget drain that lost a prompt when the sidecar answered `turn_already_running` cannot lose it now, because that answer is no longer produced for a live turn. **User-visible:** mid-response, Enter sends and the message appears in the transcript immediately, then the model picks it up at its next step instead of after the response; the Queued row is now reachable only while a session is still spawning, so its second copy variant is deleted and it always reads `Sends when the session is ready.`; the one-queued-prompt-at-a-time cap likewise applies only to the spawn park — mid-turn every Enter goes out, matching the terminal's N-deep queue. **Tests:** the four CC-26 guards that pinned "mid-turn parks" were REWRITTEN to pin the new contract (3 in `composerState.test.ts`, 1 in `App.test.tsx`, the last now asserting the removed copy can never come back), plus three sidecar boundary tests — a mid-turn submit is queued not refused (no error frame, no second turn, right mode/priority/agentId, user message broadcast once), a leftover prompt gets its own turn announced exactly once, and a queued prompt blocks an idle park. **Red-before-green by bounded mutation, restored byte-identical (sha `08d4f155…` / `f7f4fbeb…`):** flipping `announcePrompt` to true fails the announced-once test; reverting `planSessionSubmit` to the CC-26 hold fails 2 composer tests. **Battery:** `bun test app/` **2745/0** (187 files, 30.6s; baseline 2742/0) · `bun run --cwd app typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` scoped pass (5,560 upstream ignored, **0 owned**) · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `renderer:build` ✓ · `git diff --check` clean · em-dash sweep leaves JSX/code comments only · stale-copy sweep for `Sends when this response finishes.` clean across `app/`, `src/`, `web/` (remaining hits are this row, the ledger, dated review docs, and the negative test assertion). **§0 fidelity — ➕ real-added, NOT prototype parity, unchanged from CC-26's flag:** the prototype drops a mid-turn send outright (`~/catcode_prototype/cat-app/Chat.jsx:631`) and has no queued row; the anchor is the terminal engine, which is what the operator was describing both times. **⬜ Known gap, disclosed not hidden:** the injected prompt is persisted by the engine as an ATTACHMENT, and `QueryEngine` consumes attachments rather than yielding them (`src/QueryEngine.ts:949`), so the live transcript row comes from the sidecar's own broadcast and a mid-turn message may be missing when the session is later reloaded from engine history. Closing that needs a history-projection decision, not a renderer patch. **Post-implementation review (2 agents), five findings fixed in a follow-up commit:** (1) **the boundary drain could lose a prompt silently** — it dequeued and called `startTurn` with no `onRejected`/`onSettled`, so a turn that rejected before durable acceptance dropped a message the user had already watched leave the composer; it now requeues on a pre-persistence failure and retries exactly ONCE (`startTurn`'s finalizer schedules the next drain, so an unconditional requeue would spin at microtask speed), then logs and gives up. (2) **T7 depth cap added** (`MAX_QUEUED_PROMPTS = 32`, `app/shared/limits.ts`) — the per-frame and per-window caps bound arrival RATE, and the old refusal was what bounded DEPTH; queueing removed that, so a flooding renderer could pile unbounded prompt text into a queue the next tool round injects wholesale. Beyond the cap the submit is refused `bad_request` and never reaches the transcript. (3) **a validated `goalSnapshot` was silently discarded on the mid-turn branch** — the queue turns a command into an attachment, not a submit, so there is nowhere for session identity to ride; it now fails closed rather than dropping a field it just validated (latent either way, the renderer sends none). (4) `scheduleTaskNotificationDrain` renamed `scheduleBoundaryDrain` at all five call sites, since it now drains prompts first, and the task-notification predicate was extracted alongside `isDeliverableParentPrompt` instead of being inlined three times. (5) this row's Stop wording, below. **Three findings judged FALSE POSITIVE, with reasons:** an abort inside the engine's snapshot-to-`removeFromQueue` window can re-deliver, but `src/query.ts:1699` is shared with the terminal so that is parity, not a desktop defect; the durable-acceptance latch moving to frame receipt is safe because every path out of a queued prompt supplies a human turn (either the running turn consumed and persisted it, or the boundary drain starts one); and the requeue-to-tail ordering inversion is unreachable, as nothing awaits between the drain gate and `startTurn`. **Three new sidecar tests** cover the cap, the `goalSnapshot` refusal, and retry-once-then-give-up; the last was mutation-verified (deleting the requeue turns it red). **⚠ Stop, corrected from the original wording below:** pressing Stop while a message is queued does not cancel that message. The abort ends the turn, the boundary drain sees the queued prompt, and a NEW turn starts with it. That is terminal parity — Esc there cancels the query (`useCancelRequest.ts:97-101`) and leaves the queue, which `useQueueProcessor` then runs — but it means Stop can produce a turn the user did not expect, and it is worth an explicit operator check. **⬜ Still owed from CC-26:** a queued prompt cannot be cancelled (the terminal pops one with Esc, `useCancelRequest.ts:104-110`). **Layer honesty:** the renderer suite is SSR-only and the sidecar tests use a stub adapter, so this proves the frame handling, the queue placement and the submit decisions, NOT a live engine actually consuming the attachment mid-turn. **⬜ Operator GUI acceptance:** start a turn that will run several tools, type a message while it runs and press Enter — it must appear in the transcript at once, and the model must act on it BEFORE the response ends, not in a turn afterwards. Then repeat against a turn that answers with text only and confirm the message still runs, once, straight after. |
| **CC-28 · An intentionally parked session announced itself as an unexpected crash, and made the user press Restart to keep talking** → `app/main/mainDecisions.ts`, `app/main/idleParkDriver.ts`, `app/main/main.ts`, `app/supervisor/supervisor.ts`, `app/renderer/src/connectionState.ts`, `app/renderer/src/composerState.ts`, `app/renderer/src/tabStatus.ts`, `app/renderer/src/App.tsx`, `app/preload/preload.ts`, `app/shared/protocol.ts`, `docs/migration/decisions/IDLE-PARK.md` | 🟡 **CODE-COMPLETE 2026-08-05** (shared dirty `migration`, **UNCOMMITTED** by operator instruction) · headless green · real-sidecar probe green · ⬜ **operator GUI acceptance PENDING** · ⬜ **four descriptor-derived surfaces still say "crashed" (IDLE-PARK §1b)** | **Operator report:** a parked session displayed `This session stopped unexpectedly. Restart it to keep working.` **Three rulings**, all recorded as amendments in `decisions/IDLE-PARK.md` (§1a/§3a/§4a): park must not present as a failure; a session on screen must not be parked under the user; park/restore should be invisible backend behaviour, so a submit restores rather than a button. §1's explicitly-accepted "visually indistinguishable from a crashed one" consequence is **WITHDRAWN**; §10 decision 4 is **re-resolved (a) → (b)**. **Root cause — the classification was right and was then overwritten.** The exit code always reached the renderer inside `LifecycleFrame.exit`, but `supervisorEventToServerFrame` minted a lifecycle frame for BOTH the supervisor's `exit` event AND its following `status:'exited'`, and the supervisor sets that status inside its own `child.on('exit')` handler right after emitting the exit (`supervisor.ts:280-281`). Every death therefore produced the informative frame carrying `code:5` and then a **code-less copy**, which always landed last in a last-write-wins reducer (`connectionState.ts`). No single-layer test could see it: every layer was individually correct. The duplicate is no longer minted — it never carried information the first lacked, and it also made main run its terminal transcript-persist + replay-evict **twice** per death. **Fix, by plane.** *Renderer:* a renderer-local `'parked'` connection status classified off the SAME `PARKED_EXIT_CODE` the host classifies on — no protocol change, no frame kind, no descriptor field, no inbound vocabulary. It is **non-terminal**, so the existing single partition gives it a neutral tone and no recovery sentence by construction; `'parked'` joins preview and in-flight-spawn as the third `connectPending` reason so the composer stays editable; `resolvePendingSubmit` gains a `'restore'` arm so the drain calls the SAME `bridge.restoreSession` a click would and keeps holding, then sends off the resumed session's own `ready` frame. The tab drops its danger dot and its Restart button (`tabStatus.ts`, reusing the `busy` tone a preview pane already uses). *Main:* `parseVisibleSessions` boundary validation + `protectedSessions` on the park driver. *Preload:* one fixed one-way sender, `reportVisibleSessions` / `CH_HOST_VISIBLE_SESSIONS`. **"Open" resolved to VISIBLE WORKSPACE PANELS** (1 unsplit, N in a split), not every tab: tab membership is granted to any `!restorable` descriptor and a live session is never restorable, so **every live session has a tab** — exempting tabs would exempt every possible victim and switch the feature off, losing the measured ~223 MB/session reclaim. Protection removes a session from the victim POOL only; it is still counted for the cap, so it redirects the sweep rather than raising the engine ceiling. **Security:** the hint names sessions to EXEMPT from an optimisation — it starts nothing, addresses nothing, reaches no sidecar, and carries no path, policy, permission id or engine object; unknown ids are inert. Worst case for a hostile renderer is that main declines to park, i.e. it retains its own RAM. Host control plane, **not** sidecar vocabulary: no inbound frame kind, no `checkStrictKeys` entry, no socket-boundary schema. Validated at main (array-of-strings, per-id length bound, capped at `MAX_LIVE_SESSIONS`; malformed ⇒ empty set ⇒ less protection, never a failed IPC). Hardening bridge-method inventory updated. **Honest failures preserved:** only `code:5` on an `exited` frame is a park; a `disconnected`/`failed` frame is never reclassified whatever it carries; a real crash, a signal kill, a plain zero exit and a `RESUME_FAILED_EXIT_CODE` death all still read `exited` with the danger tone and the sentence. `'parked'` is deliberately **not** sticky — a re-spawn that dies wins with its own status — because a "once parked, stay parked" latch would have hidden exactly that case. A refused restore releases the prompt back into the composer with the typed reason. **Exactly-once delivery** rides mechanisms already there: `alreadyParked` ⇒ `ignore` (two prompts cannot queue), `claimLazyRestore` (two restores cannot spawn; a park clears the claim, so park N+1 restores again). **Tests — the load-bearing one is a real-lifecycle probe, not a shape assertion:** `app/main/idleParkLifecycle.probe.test.ts` runs a real Bun sidecar under the real registry/supervisor/`Host`/`createIdleParkDriver`, parks it over the real socket, folds the REAL event stream through main's real translation and the renderer's real reducer, and asserts the composed result — including that no terminal frame follows the classification. **It fails on the pre-fix code with `Expected: "parked" / Received: "exited"`**, which is the reported defect reproduced end to end. It also proves the visible-pane protection against a live engine (sweep twice while visible ⇒ process alive; unprotect ⇒ parks) and that the unpark is a genuine resume (both `ready` frames echo the same `engineSessionId`). Plus unit coverage: park-vs-every-other-exit-code, no-smuggling into non-exit statuses, a restore that dies after a park, `connectionHasEngine`, the parked composer gate, the parked drain arm, five driver-protection cases, and five `parseVisibleSessions` cases. **One rider, disclosed:** `isTerminalConnectionStatus` had been standing in for "can I send to this session?", and `'parked'` is the first status where those come apart (non-terminal, no process). Left alone, the context-breakdown popover would have sent a verb into the void, got `session_not_found`, and raised a false `dead` — re-opening the bug commit `6971cde` fixed. New `connectionHasEngine` predicate with its own exhaustiveness tripwire; the gate now asks it. **⬜ INCOMPLETE, found by post-implementation review (3 agents), NOT fixed — recorded in `decisions/IDLE-PARK.md` §1b:** §1a fixed the surfaces that read the renderer's CONNECTION snapshot, but every OTHER session surface is descriptor-derived through the shared `sessionStatusVisual`, which has no parked input and maps `disconnected`+`restorable` to `{tone:'dead', label:'crashed'}` — so a parked session still shows a red `crashed` chip on the Sessions page, `CRASHED` in the ⌘K palette, `crashed` in the Sidebar aria-label, and a debug export that contradicts itself (`sidebar[]` crashed vs `tabs[]` idle for the same session) while that export is the GUI-VERIFICATION cross-check source. Mechanism: `tabStatus.ts` returns its parked visual BEFORE calling `sessionStatusVisual`, so a fifth label+tone decision lives outside the module whose header says it "replaces FOUR drifted copies of the same switch". Closing it is a fork — (A) thread a parked signal through ~7 renderer modules incl. `MergedSessionRow`, or (B) add `parked` to `SessionDescriptor` (one field, set where `row.shutdown === 'parked'` is already computed, all five surfaces inherit it) which REVERSES §11. §11 rejected (B) to keep "the renderer provably unaware park exists"; the 2026-08-05 ruling withdrew that goal, so its premise is void — but reversing a recorded decision is an operator call, so it is written down, not done. **Honest statement: a park no longer presents as a failure in the chat view; it still does elsewhere.** **Also fixed from the same review:** the visible-set report recorded its dedupe key BEFORE the send, so one call throttled by the preload's shared rate guard could pin main to a stale protected set (including failing to protect the pane just switched to); the empty set was unreportable (`useRef('')` vs an empty join key), so a renderer reload left main protecting pre-reload ids; the report ignored `activeView`, so sitting on Settings exempted every session from the TTL indefinitely; and an unparking tab painted `idle` for the several seconds of a real engine boot instead of `starting`. Three App.tsx tripwires added for the glue an SSR suite cannot execute (the drain's `restore` arm, the visible-set effect's panel source + chat gate + send-before-record ordering). Stale routing invariant in `docs/maps/web-app-runtime.md` corrected: it said "Do not add a preload channel" for idle-park, which now reads as violated by its own feature. **§0 flag (proposal, OPEN):** the parked tab's aria-only status word is **`idle`** — one plain-English word chosen because every existing label (`crashed`/`closed`/`disconnected`/`preview`) would misstate it; no new tone, colour or chip vocabulary was added, and the TabBar renders a dot, not the word. Related: the status-chip system is already tracked as invented drift with its own owner. **Round-2 review (3 bug-focused agents, adversarial): FOUR more real bugs, all fixed.** (a) **The sharpest edge of making park non-terminal, and a regression of this change:** `pendingSubmits` was cleared only by a send or a release, and a session closed while parked emits NO lifecycle frame (the host deregisters the supervisor record before the child dies, so the exit is dropped), so its connection stayed `'parked'` forever, the drain kept answering `'restore'` on every pass, and the app **re-spawned a real billed engine for a tab the user had closed and sent the message into it**. Before park existed the same stale entry was inert (`'exited'` is terminal → released on the first pass). `closeTab` and the `session-removed` handler now release, and `restoreParkedSession` refuses a session owning neither a tab nor a preview. (b) **A second, earlier copy of the headline defect, proved with a real sidecar trace:** the sidecar closes its socket inside `cleanup()` BEFORE `process.exit`, so the supervisor saw FIN ~7 ms ahead of the reap and minted a transport `disconnected` — so EVERY park flashed "This session lost its connection. Restart it to reconnect." plus a Restart button for one render before the code-5 frame corrected it. A socket close and a process death are the same event seen twice: `supervisor.reportSocketLoss` now settles briefly and drops the report if the record reached a terminal state meanwhile (`disconnectSettleMs`, default 250 ms); `record.socket` is still cleared synchronously so `send` fails fast unchanged, and a genuine drop under a LIVING child still reports (both pinned by new supervisor tests). (c) **One stray verb from a parked pane turned the park straight back into a crash:** `supervisor.send` answers `session_disconnected` for the tombstone (or `session_not_found` once deregistered), which the reducer mapped to a terminal status — restoring the danger banner, the Restart button, the read-only composer and releasing the held prompt, in one click. The composer is deliberately LIVE on a parked session now, so it was reachable: the agent-mode toggle is the one dispatcher supplied unconditionally (every sibling goes dead when its per-session snapshot nulls out). Both halves fixed: the reducer refuses to downgrade `'parked'` on those two no-engine codes (`session_not_ready` is deliberately NOT absorbed — it means the unpark is spawning), and the toggle is gated on `connectionHasEngine`. (d) **A session parked before it ever ran a turn has no transcript, so restore AND restart both fail** — the tab read `idle` with no affordance, converting a visible failure into a silent one while every Enter destroyed the paste pills via `retireDraft`. Presentation fixed (`tabStatus.ts` now gates on `descriptor.restorable`); the root fix is open (§1c). **Also fixed:** `restoreParkedSession` had already drifted from `restoreLiveSession` — it lost the `cancelledRestoresRef` handshake and a release path — so it now DELEGATES with a `focus:false` flag instead of forking the protocol; and **one of my own new tripwires passed vacuously** (`activeView === 'chat'` was matched by the explanatory comment directly above the code, so replacing the real condition with `true` left it green — caught by agent mutation-testing, now comment-stripped and mutation-verified by me). **The probe was blind to half of this:** it asserted only what came AFTER the park frame. It now asserts the death produces EXACTLY ONE lifecycle frame; setting `disconnectSettleMs: 0` turns it red with `Expected: 1 / Received: 2`, which is the fail-before/pass-after proof for (b). **⬜ Round-2 items NOT fixed (recorded, not hidden):** §1c root fix (never park a session that cannot come back — needs the host's private `canResume` exposed to the park path); a pre-existing `host.closeSession`-racing-`spawn` window that can leave a live engine behind a `clean` row (needs per-session lifecycle serialization at the host); and a renderer reload briefly reporting an empty visible set while the roster hydrates (fail-safe direction: less protection, self-corrects in ms). **Battery (re-run after both review rounds):** `bun test app/` **2742/0** (baseline 2707/0) · app `typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` scoped pass (**0 owned**) · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `renderer:build` ✓ · `maps:lint` passed · `git diff --check` clean. **Layer honesty:** the renderer suite is SSR-only, so the probe proves the lifecycle, the classification and the drain decisions, NOT the keystroke or the React effect that fires the restore. **⬜ Operator GUI acceptance:** open two sessions, leave one visible and idle past 20 minutes and confirm it is NOT parked; switch away from it, wait for the sweep, then return and confirm the tab shows no failure dot, no Restart button, no banner, a readable transcript and a usable composer; type a message and press Enter once — the engine must come back on its own and the message must be delivered exactly once, with the prior conversation still above it. |
