D2 ruled the `grouped` MESSAGE TYPE cut while grouping survives "as a projector/selector-level grouping over correlated tool rows, zero new frame types" (`decisions/AGENT-CHROME.md:75`). **Four ledger rows were blocked on this seam and are now closed:** §05 `GroupedToolGroup` (✂ cut, "the promised grouping-as-projector-derivation is NOT yet implemented … has no named owner"), §05 `ReadGroupRow` (⬜ deferred — blocked-on-seam), §05 `GrepCard` (grouped-pattern counts deferred), §06 `selectReadGroups`. **Built:** `groupToolRuns` in a new `toolRunLayout.ts` — a READ-TIME derivation over already-projected rows, same C3 discipline as `groupAgentDelegates` and `groupReasoningRuns` (same WeakMap input-reference cache, same pass-through-by-reference, returns the INPUT array when nothing groups so upstream identity survives). A run carries its `family`, and the view dispatches on it exactly as `GroupedToolGroup` dispatches on `groupKind`. **The grouping rule is the engine's own, not invented:** `collapseReadSearchGroups` (`src/utils/collapseReadSearch.ts:762`) accumulates consecutive collapsible calls and flushes on assistant text or a non-collapsible tool use; every non-groupable display item is that break here. That module could not be imported — it is the terminal plane (engine `RenderableMessage` + the `Tools` registry + `bun:bundle` feature gates) — so the RULE is mirrored and cited, not duplicated wholesale. **One deliberate narrowing vs the engine:** it puts reads and searches in ONE accumulator with a combined summary; the prototype has a separate card per kind and so does this, so a run CHANGES family rather than merging two kinds under one head. **Which families group, checked against both authorities rather than assumed — only read and grep:** edit/write are in the engine's `isNonCollapsibleToolUse` (`collapseReadSearch.ts:345-368`), so an edit deliberately BREAKS a collapse group, and §05 `MultiDiffCard` already cut the prototype's many-files edit switcher because `FileEditTool` returns ONE file's patch; a collapsed edit card also already carries its filename and ±counts, real information a run would hide. Bash is collapsible in the engine only under fullscreen env; web/notebook/lsp/skill/agent have neither prototype nor engine backing. **Grep digest — `grepResult.ts`, a parser module in the `readSource.ts` mould:** the structured `numFiles`/`numLines`/`numMatches` never leave the tool (`GrepTool.ts:146-151`; `mapToolResultToToolResultBlockParam` `:254` flattens them into prose), so the per-pattern count is parsed back off the exact strings that function writes. **Three modes report three different UNITS** (`GrepTool.ts:52-56`) and `files_with_matches` — which counts FILES — is the DEFAULT, so labelling everything "matches" would misreport the common case: the member row says what its own mode measured (`3 files` / `6 matches` / `no matches`), and the run's sub totals ONLY when every member agrees on the unit, else falls back to the pattern count rather than summing files into matches. Context lines (`path-N-`) are excluded from match counts so an `-A`/`-B`/`-C` window cannot inflate them. **Renderer-only: no frame kind, preload channel, inbound vocabulary, sidecar, protocol, or security-boundary change; zero projector rows mutated.** **User-visible:** adjacent reads become one `≡ Read · N files` card that HOISTS the directory the run shares onto the head and lists each file with only what differs (rows read e.g. `renderer/src/TranscriptView.tsx` and `shared/protocol.ts`), the remaining directory dimmed and the filename at full strength — the directory is what truncates, since ellipsizing from the right would eat the one part that tells two same-named files apart. `commonDirPrefix` (`pathUtils.ts`) hoists only a whole shared directory segment and never a bare root, so a run spanning two roots keeps entire paths on its rows. A ranged read also states its range (`, lines 812 to 861`), which the prototype's row does (`Messages.jsx:1070`). Adjacent searches become one `⌕ Search · N patterns` card listing each pattern with its own count. Every member expands to the SHARED `ToolCardBody`, so it inherits the highlighted read body, the per-file search coloring, the truncation reveal band and the inspector route a standalone card has. A LONE call is untouched. **§0 flags (proposals, OPEN and unapproved):** (a) 🔁 adapted — the run reports the WORST member outcome (any error, then any pending, else done) and a non-success member shows the shared state cluster on its own row; the prototype's grouped items are fixtures with no state to fold (`Messages.jsx:1060`, `:686-690`). (b) 🔁 adapted — a failed member opens itself through the same `resolveToolCardExpanded` rule the cards use. (c) 🔁 adapted — SEARCH members expand at all: the prototype's grep rows are inert (pattern + count, no caret, no click, `:686-691`) because its fixture carries no output, but real search rows do and the results are the entire point, so an inert row would make grouping a way to LOSE every result. (d) ➕ real-added — `toolsExpanded` is threaded to the member, so "tools open by default" still reaches the call it used to open before grouping existed; **this was caught by a failing test, not by review** — with it missing, an opened search run showed counts and no results. Now implemented as approved, with `commonDirPrefix` unit-tested for the degenerate cases the mockup never exercised. Also fixed: the read digest counted lines on payloads that carry NO file (an empty file, an offset past EOF, an unchanged file all SUCCEED with a `<system-reminder>`/stub body, and a memory-file read prepends a freshness line that breaks the numbering, `FileReadTool.ts:685-703`), so `Read 1 line` could appear for a 0-line file — it now reports only when `parseReadSource` really recognised the numbered shape, which is what its own doc comment already promised; every grep payload was parsed TWICE per member per frame (head total + row digest) with neither component memoized on a path the repo already fixed once for `ReasoningStep`, so digests are now computed once in the card and passed down and `ToolRunRow` is `memo`; an all-empty search run rendered identically to the mixed-unit refusal, conflating a measured zero with "these do not add up"; the reasoning head printed `steps.length` while the list showed the last 1000, so it now reads `N of M steps` when the ceiling bites; the prototype's per-member line range was dropped with no flag and is now rendered; `dirname`'s round-trip doc claim was false for trailing separators and is narrowed; the `runFamily === null` branch in `flush` was unreachable, so family now lives inside the run object and cannot drift. **Two bugs in the review fixes were caught by tests I wrote for them, not by inspection:** `commonDirPrefix` emitted a doubled separator when every member shared its ENTIRE directory, and rejoining segments with `/` corrupted Windows paths into something that prefixed nothing — the prefix is now cut from the original string. **The review's one DEFERRED finding was then fixed in the same session** (`app/renderer/src/toolCardExpansion.ts`): forming a run remounted an open single card, so a read the user expanded mid-turn snapped shut the moment the next adjacent read arrived — both the key (`<row id>` → `read-run:<row id>`) and the component type (`ToolCard` → `ToolRunRow`) change, so React unmounted the tree and local `useState` died with it. Card expansion now lives in a store owned ABOVE the derivations, keyed on the engine-minted `toolUseId`, which is the one handle that survives a row being re-projected, re-nested, or folded into a run. A Map behind a ref, NOT React state: writing through state would re-render the whole transcript on every card toggle, when the only component that must react is the one clicked. `ToolCardShell`, `AgentToolCard` and `ToolRunRow` all key on their row's `toolUseId`; the run HEAD keys on `run:<first member toolUseId>`, which does not move as the run grows, so a two-read run the user opened stays open at six. A missing provider degrades to per-instance memory (the branch exists for a bare mount; it is asserted at the store level, not through a rendered click). **The first version of this fix was incomplete, and my own test caught it:** the expansion survived, but the new run head was collapsed by default, so the user's file was remembered and still invisible — which is the same complaint. A run now opens when it holds a member the user explicitly opened (`useAnyToolCardOpened`, guarded on `=== true` so a member they deliberately CLOSED does not drag the head open). **A second test was passing vacuously** and was rewritten: it asserted the body text `one`, which is a substring of the status word `done`. **A second review pass then found four more defects in this work, all fixed.** (a) The per-member range label was OFF BY ONE and its comment cited the source backwards: `offset` is ONE-based (`FileReadTool.ts:229,496,1036` — default 1, and it becomes `startLine`, which is what `addLineNumbers` prints), so a `offset:811` read was labelled `lines 812 to 861` directly above a gutter reading 811. The label now states `offset` itself, and the test that enshrined the bug carried an invented payload the engine would never emit. (b) The R5 fix had reintroduced its own bug one level up: the run head opened only because a member was open, and that was RE-DERIVED every render, so closing the member that held the run open collapsed the entire run a frame later, siblings and all. A member toggle now pins its run open in the store (you can only reach a member while the run is open, so the click is itself the evidence), and the derived value decides only the first render after a regroup. (c) The digest work claimed in a comment to parse each payload once and did the opposite: `toolRunHead`'s `digests` parameter was never read and it re-derived every grep digest, so payloads were parsed twice per render — and a COLLAPSED run parsed every member's whole file on every frame, measured at 37ms for two 40k-line members. Digests are now cached on the RESULT object (`WeakMap`), which is the right key because rows are rebuilt each frame while `result` is copied by reference; the dead parameter is gone and the comment is now true. The `memo` comment was corrected too: it buys little during streaming for the same reason. (d) `commonDirPrefix` could return a prefix that did not prefix every input on a mixed-separator run; the invariant is now total. Also added: a closed-union tripwire on `deriveToolRunStatus`, a `limit <= 0` guard, and the first tests of the 1000-step ceiling. **A THIRD vacuous test was caught and replaced** — `without a provider a card still toggles` rendered through `TranscriptRowsView`, which always installs a provider, so it never entered the branch it named and its one assertion was the default state. Replaced with store-level unit tests plus a pair that pin the run-collapse guard from both sides. To make any of this provable without a DOM, `TranscriptRowsView` now prefers an ancestor's store over creating its own — a real composability property (a future owner can lift expansion above this view) that also lets a test drive one store through two different row shapes. 5 new SSR tests: survives-the-regroup, keyed-by-tool-use-id, run head keeps its own expansion as the run grows, closed-member guard, no-provider fallback. **⬜ Operator GUI acceptance:** trigger a turn that reads several files at once and one that runs several Grep calls in a row; confirm one card each reading `N files` / `N patterns`, that read rows show their directory and search rows their own count, that clicking a row opens that call's body, and that a batch containing a missing file shows `failed` on the head with the failing row already open. |

Stop : Send`), so Enter is the mid-turn submit path; the existing Queued row now names which wait it is in (`Sends when this response finishes.` vs `Sends when the session is ready.`); `PENDING_SUBMIT_RELEASED_MESSAGE` dropped its `The session did not connect` clause, which a turn-queued prompt released by a mid-turn crash would have stated falsely. **§0 fidelity — ➕ real-added, NOT prototype parity:** the prototype DROPS a mid-turn send outright (`~/catcode_prototype/cat-app/Chat.jsx:631`, `if (!text || isGenerating) return;`) and has no queued row at all. This change makes that reachable: the sidecar can start its own task-notification turn microtasks after the boundary (`sidecarServer.ts:1054`, scheduled at `:1162`), and it then answers `turn_already_running` (`:1252`) — a code the renderer maps nowhere (`connectionState.ts:174-186` handles three codes; `rawMessageLog.ts:113-119` just shows the raw engine string, itself a §7 violation). | **CC-25 · The whole in-turn activity surface was built but never rendered (turn state was an attach-only handshake value)** → `src/app-runtime/sessionEvents.ts`, `src/app-runtime/AppSessionController.ts`, `src/web/appSessionEventMapper.ts`, `app/shared/engine-types.snapshot.d.ts`, `app/renderer/src/connectionState.ts`, `app/renderer/src/rawMessageLog.ts` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-08-02** (local `migration`) · headless + real-socket verified · ⬜ **operator GUI acceptance PENDING** | **Investigation trigger:** operator asked what the app actually renders while the agent is working. **Root cause:** `SessionPane` gates every running-state affordance on `generating = status==='ready' && !inputEnabled` (`App.tsx:3359`), but `inputEnabled` was only ever written from the `app.ready` handshake (`connectionState.ts:128`, `rawMessageLog.ts:78`), which the sidecar sends exactly once at attach (`sidecarServer.ts:498`). The sidecar flipped its own `activeTurn` at `:1066`/`:1097` and never re-broadcast, and no frame in `protocol.ts` carried turn state — so `generating` was **false for the entire lifetime of a normally-attached session**. Proven before the fix by replaying the repo's own `S1_STREAMING_TEXT_TURN` fixture through the real reducers: `generating=false` at all 9 steps. **Dead as a result:** the activity indicator (verb/target/elapsed/token byline, `App.tsx:3831`), its **Stop** button (its only mount site), **Esc-to-interrupt** (`App.tsx:3622`), the jump-to-bottom running cue (`:3745`), the mid-turn composer read-only + "Input is unavailable…" placeholder (all of P4-58's deliverable, `:3379`), and the amber paused state. **Why CI never caught it:** the renderer suite is SSR-only and fed `inputEnabled:false` directly as a prop (`App.test.tsx:883`), so it asserted the render of a state nothing could produce. This is the `run-it-before-claiming-done` failure class again, at the wire layer. **Fix — the engine announces its own turn boundary, rather than `app/` duplicating it** (CLAUDE.md §8 rule 10): new `AppSessionTurnStatusEvent` (`turn.status`) on the engine's `AppSessionEvent` union, emitted by a private `setActiveTurn` setter that is now the ONLY writer of `activeTurn` — modelled exactly on the existing `setAbortState`→`emit` precedent, which was already doing this correctly for abort. No new frame kind and no sidecar broadcast plumbing was needed: `sidecarServer.ts:395` already forwards every controller event. Renderer reduces it into `inputEnabled` in both stores (they must move together — `selectComposerGate` reads both). Turn events move `inputEnabled` only, never `status`, so a late frame cannot revive a dead session; repeated values return the identical state object. **Live-path proof (the point):** a new real-spawn probe in `roundtrip.probe.test.ts` asserts `turn.status` crosses a real Unix-domain socket, arrives after `ready`, opens before the turn's first message and closes after its last. **Battery:** `bun test app/` **2307/0** (169 files, 8060 assertions; 2305/0 at session start) · app `typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` scoped pass (5,559 upstream ignored, **0 owned**) · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `renderer:build` ✓ · engine `build:dev:full` green (`2.1.87-dev.20260802.t100719.sha312c50c5`) · focused engine `src/app-runtime/` **19/0** + `src/web/` **38/0**. **Tripwires fired and were resolved, not silenced:** five existing event-order assertions changed because the new event genuinely appears on the real path. One was a security test (F6, secret-in-outbound-event) whose `no event frame at all` assertion the benign boundary event would have loosened — it was **strengthened** instead, now pinning that the raw wire bytes contain neither `sk-leak` nor `accessToken`. **No protocol version bump, no new inbound vocabulary, no new preload channel, no secret-boundary change** — outbound-only, additive, and the payload is one boolean. **⬜ NOT fixed, separate (flagged, not dropped):** (a) thinking still never streams — `projectStreamEvent` drops every non-`text_delta` delta (`transcriptProjector.ts:1452`), so a long reasoning phase remains a blank transcript after the user bubble; that file is dirty in another session. (b) the elapsed clock resets on session switch (`App.tsx:3428` keys on `activeSessionId`). (c) `deriveActivity` uses `toolName` as the target (`appModel.ts:55`), so the row reads "Running Bash" not the actual command. (b) and (c) only became observable once this landed. **⬜ Known stale, deliberately untouched:** `scripts/typecheck/renderer-engine-types/engine-types.snapshot.d.ts` is a P1-0-era copy already missing the entire `AppClientMessage`/`AppReadyPayload` section; it is a temporary harness CLAUDE.md §1 says not to extend, and nothing gates on it. **⬜ Operator GUI acceptance:** send a turn and confirm the activity row appears above the composer with a live elapsed clock, Stop and Esc both interrupt, the composer goes read-only mid-turn and recovers after, and scrolling up shows the running cue instead of "↓ Latest". ⚠ **The read-only-mid-turn step is SUPERSEDED by CC-26 2026-08-02 — skip it:** the composer is now deliberately typeable mid-turn and queues the submit, so a read-only composer would be the failure, not the pass. | **CC-22 · Internal `No response requested.` leaked into desktop transcript (`/compact` plus silent fallback paths)** → `src/utils/conversationRecovery.ts`, `src/utils/queryHelpers.ts`, `src/utils/messages/mappers.ts`, `app/sidecar/historyProjection.ts` | ✅ **CODE-COMPLETE 2026-08-01** (shared dirty `migration`, uncommitted) · headless verified | **Root causes:** recovery appended an untyped assistant string after the trailing `/compact` stdout to preserve API alternation; interruption detection treated that stdout as a user prompt; and the silent rate-limit fallback independently created the same text as an API-error record. **Fix:** current internal records carry `isInternalNoResponseSentinel`; recovery keeps its marker in model-facing resumed state while terminal, live SDK normalization, the general internal→SDK mapper, and desktop history projection omit it. Legacy persisted fallback records are recognized only by `isApiErrorMessage` plus the exact sentinel-only payload, so a genuine assistant response with identical text remains visible. Interruption detection scans past compact summaries, meta rows, and local-command caveat/name/message/args/stdout/stderr bookkeeping to the last semantic turn, so compact-only or other command-bookkeeping tails report no unanswered prompt while a real trailing prompt keeps `interrupted_prompt`. The engine seed still receives only compacted context; no pre-compaction rows are reintroduced. CC-23's bounded archival-prefix/exact-seed-tail display projection retains its existing caps and merge rules. No protocol shape, inbound vocabulary, secret boundary, replay frame/byte cap, or reload budget changed. **Regression coverage:** compact-only recovery/API validity; genuine trailing prompt; current + legacy silent fallback; live SDK suppression; general replay suppression; genuine identical-text controls; compact-boundary replay; real Unix-socket resume in the full suite. **Verification:** focused **20/0**; full `bun test app/` **2,199/0** (7,775 assertions, 165 files); app tsc clean; scoped sidecar tsc green (5,559 upstream ignored, 0 owned); hardening **19/19**; `build:dev:full` green (`2.1.87-dev.20260801.t063520.sha8bb244e2`, maps lint 8 existing recommendations). **§0 / fidelity:** none; no production visual surface or protocol/security decision changed. |

Three consequences: (1) no session ⇒ no data; (2) N sessions ⇒ N in-memory copies ⇒ different numbers for the same account; (3) **fatal for a usage page** — `refreshAccountsUsageOnce()` is guarded one-shot per sidecar (`sidecarServer.ts:2569`) and after that re-broadcasts only on that session's own pool-mutating verb, so the page showed the number its session loaded at spawn, forever. **Landed:** accounts is now a host-plane read, structurally identical to `CATALOG-OWNERSHIP` shape (b) and reusing its machinery — disposable engine-graph worker → bounded NDJSON → main-side fail-closed parse + `secretGuard` re-scan → read-only `accounts-pool` host event → global slot in `accountsState`. It instead uses the engine's OWN observation-only entry point `loadPoolForObservation()` (`codexAccountPool.ts:171-181`, extracted upstream for exactly this caller class: "without triggering token refresh, quarantine probes, or usage polls") plus `enableConfigs()`; `initializeRuntime.ts` was split to export the MACRO shim alone rather than duplicate it. **Caught only by running it:** the first build failed live with `[accounts-worker] fatal: Config accessed before allowed` (`config.ts:1465`) — the observation path still reads global config; surfaced by the hardening smoke actually spawning the worker, not by any unit test. **Verified end-to-end against the REAL vault** (read-only): one record, 2,902 B, 5 Codex + 2 Anthropic accounts with live usage, parses through the main-side boundary, `secretGuard` clean, and zero occurrences of `accessToken`/`refreshToken`/`vaultFilePath`/`idToken` in the raw bytes. **Tests:** 34 new (16 boundary accept/reject incl. a token-smuggling row rejected outright, 13 runner accept/fail-closed/driver-cadence, 5 renderer covering the exact regression — pool populates with `state.sessions === {}`, global outranks session, session teardown never clears it). **Battery:** `bun test app/` **1709 pass / 0 fail** (was 1675 pre-change) · `typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` 0 owned (5546 upstream ignored) · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `renderer:build` ok · `maps:lint` passed · `git diff --check` clean. **Security:** outbound-only on the EXISTING `catcode:host:event` channel — no new preload channel, no new inbound vocabulary, no protocol version bump; writes stay session-plane `account.*` verbs with their T6 re-resolve intact. **Also fixed:** the waiting-state copy printed engineering notes at the user ("does not use prototype fixtures… once the sidecar sends the real redacted Codex account pool"), the §7 / `DeferredNote` violation; now "Loading accounts…", with a test asserting the empty state contains none of `sidecar`/`prototype`/`redacted`/`snapshot`. **⬜ NOT closed (flagged, not faked):** the prototype's usage-ANALYTICS half — 4-col stat grid, Tokens-by-Account / Daily-Tokens-by-Model series with the 7d/30d toggle, By-Account / By-Model / Main-vs-Subagents / Cache breakdowns (`Pages.jsx:589-704`) — still does not exist. `AccountStatus` carries current-window used-percent only; there is no per-account token HISTORY anywhere on the wire and the prototype's own comments mark that region DEMO-ONLY. Needs an engine-side usage-history source = its own session. This change makes the page a live pool + headroom view, NOT the full analytics dashboard the operator's "same goal as the prototype" may still mean. **⬜ Operator GUI acceptance:** launch the app, click Accounts with NO session open — the pool must populate within a few seconds and the headroom percentages must change over a >60 s watch without touching anything. **⬜ Noticed in passing, not fixed (own task spawned):** `MemoryPage.tsx:66` carries the identical engineering-note copy. |

This row is the authority. **➕ real-added, NOT parity** — the prototype sidebar has no workspace reordering at all (its only drag is a SESSION into a split pane, `Sidebar.jsx:246`), so there is no prototype anchor; it adds no element the `PARITY-LEDGER.md` tracks, so the ledger was correctly left untouched. **Design — one pure module, thin DOM over it.** `sidebarWorkspaceOrder.ts` owns the whole decision: `selectOrderedWorkspaceGroups` (ranked-first, then the incoming frozen-alphabetical remainder, Unknown bucket pinned last), `selectWorkspaceDropEdge`, `reduceWorkspaceOrderMoved`, `reduceWorkspaceOrderStepped`, plus the versioned read/write pair. Forced by the `app/renderer` suite being SSR-only (`renderToStaticMarkup` — it cannot fire a drag, a pointer event, or an effect); `composerState.ts` (CC-16) is the precedent for the split. **Constraints honoured, verified in source:** (1) the shared `groupByWorkspace` (`sessionsCatalogState.ts:508`, used by BOTH `Sidebar.tsx:203` and `SessionsPage.tsx:37`) is byte-identical — the custom order is applied to its RESULT on the sidebar side, so the Sessions page keeps frozen-alphabetical; an order entry for an **absent** workspace is **kept but skipped** (the normal case as the search box filters groups in and out, and how a rank survives a relaunch that has not enumerated the workspace yet), so the rail degrades to alphabetical rather than throwing or reshuffling; a **new** workspace joins **after** everything the operator placed, displacing nothing; a **keyboard path exists** (⌥↑/⌥↓ on the focused header, advertised via `aria-keyshortcuts`) because drag-only is inaccessible and Alt+Arrow was unclaimed in the renderer. **Drag payload is `text/workspace-cwd`**, deliberately distinct from the tab→split `text/sessionId` (`TabBar.tsx:265`), so the two drags can never cross-trigger. **Drop indicator** is a static-class 2px accent rule (`h-[2px] bg-accent`, the TabBar-underline idiom) on the exact edge the reducer will use, plus `opacity-50` on the source — **all static classes, verified PRESENT in the built CSS** (`app/renderer/dist/assets/index-*.css`), so the Tailwind-v4 interpolated-arbitrary-value trap is avoided by construction. **Red-before-green by bounded mutation, restored byte-for-byte (sha `6e184c21…` / `c297d28e…`):** ignoring the order in `selectOrderedWorkspaceGroups` → 7 fail; Restored end-to-end: provider-neutral `/login`; combined Anthropic+Codex `/usage`; credential-aware pre-turn model options; explicit Claude selection crosses from OpenAI to first-party/Bedrock/Vertex/Foundry; provider-local Default clears the explicit model; first accepted submit and restored provider-bound history lock cross-provider changes across desktop, `/model`, and `/switch-account`; `/effort` and the desktop picker reject unsupported model/level pairs, show the effective applied tier, and reset an incompatible raw tier to Auto on model change; Fast enable repeats model/account availability gates at the boundary; resumed desktop sessions ignore synthetic/API-error tails and seed the latest real transcript model/provider; first-run authentication activates the chosen provider only after credential commit, while ordinary Add-account remains non-activating; `/switch-account <alias>` changes provider+model atomically before turn one and advertises both Claude and Codex; stale recovery copy now directs users to Anthropic sign-in; the composer shows the active Claude subscription account only when that pool authenticates the route, never for API-key/Bedrock/Vertex/Foundry routes; Accounts renders both pools, can switch healthy Anthropic accounts, reports non-pool routes honestly, and retains an explicit Add-account OAuth owner through retry/success. OAuth: `account.login {provider, activateProvider?}` is strict-schema validated; the Anthropic runner reuses `OAuthService` + `installOAuthTokens`, honors forced method/org policy, validates the org **before** credential commit, cancels safely, and refreshes accounts + model controls on success. Preserved: N-process topology, raw events, two IDs, UDS transport, preload default-deny, T4/T5a/T6/T6b/T7/HC1–HC4, and Codex lifecycle. **Live proof (post-build):** real Haiku initialized as `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` from build `2.1.87-dev.20260727.t090004.sha2320f73e`, emitted a real `Read` for this repo's `package.json`, consumed the result, and completed successfully in 2 turns as `cat-code 2.1.87`, with 0 permission denials. **Verification:** focused 326/0; full `bun test app/` **1,567/0** (135 files); app tsc clean; scoped sidecar tsc green (5,546 upstream ignored, 0 owned); renderer build green (590 modules); `build:dev:full` green; maps lint green (8 existing recommendations). **Review:** `docs/migration/reviews/2026-07-27-anthropic-restoration-usability-review.md`. **Known broader follow-ups:** Claude rename/delete/logout remain available in the CLI but are not yet row actions in the desktop; account-pool mutations are not broadcast across already-running N-process sidecars; OAuth progress is session-scoped but not request/provider-tagged. **PENDING before ✅:** `bun run --cwd app test:hardening` (launches Electron; not authorized this run) and operator-driven GUI checks for provider cards, Anthropic row/switch, Default/model/effort/Fast controls, resumed routing, and a real next-turn execution. **Fidelity:** provider choice and Anthropic roster are intentional ➕ real-added extensions authorized by this goal; visual acceptance remains open. |
| **CC-16 · Opening a not-live session made the user wait on, and act for, the engine spawn** → `app/renderer/src/App.tsx`, `app/renderer/src/composerState.ts` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-27** (local `migration`, not pushed) · headless-only · ⬜ operator GUI acceptance PENDING | **Operator report:** *"When open unconnected session. There is a small time that it show as 'connecting…' or state before focus the composer 'focus to connect'. I dont think we need it. **User should not feel anything.**"* **Root cause:** one flag, `composerEnabled` (pre-fix `App.tsx:2713-2718`), conflated two unrelated reasons the composer could be unusable — *"no engine attached yet"* (preview pane / `connecting` / `starting`) and *"the engine says input is closed"* (`ready` + `inputEnabled:false`, i.e. mid-turn). Because they were one flag, the transient case inherited the mid-turn treatment: `readOnly={!composerEnabled}` (`App.tsx:3201`) refused every keystroke for the ~0.6 s cold-connect floor, and the placeholder cascade (`App.tsx:3220-3224`) *instructed* the user — `Focus to reconnect…`, then `Connecting…`. **Fix — separate the two, then hide the spawn behind the typing the user is already doing.** New pure model in `composerState.ts`: `selectComposerGate` returns `{engineInputEnabled, connectPending, editable}`; `editable = engineInputEnabled ‖ connectPending` drives all three composer consumers (textarea `readOnly`, send arrow, attach) so "typeable" can never drift from "sendable" again. Typing, the send arrow and attach are live while connect-pending; **only the submit waits** — `planSessionSubmit` returns `hold`, `submitSession` parks the paste-EXPANDED text per session (`reducePendingSubmitHeld`, keyed like `promptDrafts`) and retires the draft exactly like a real send, and a `useEffect` drains it through the **existing** `app.submit` the moment `resolvePendingSubmit` sees ready+input-enabled. **NOT spawn-on-open** — the CC-5 §I.1 ruling-#3 dwell removal stands untouched; the spawn still fires only on intent (`engagePreviewPane` on focus/pointer-down, now also on form-submit so a send-arrow click on a pre-filled draft can't wait on a spawn nobody started). **Failure is observable, never silent:** every terminal outcome — the three failure paths of `restoreLiveSession` and any terminal connection status (`dead`/`failed`/`exited`/`disconnected`) — calls `releasePendingSubmit`, which puts the text BACK in the composer (`restoreDraftWithPending` prepends it without clobbering anything typed since) and raises `PENDING_SUBMIT_RELEASED_MESSAGE`. **Mid-turn is untouched and pinned:** two regression guards (`composerState.test.ts` gate + plan, `App.test.tsx` SSR) assert a `ready`+`inputEnabled:false` composer is still read-only with its send arrow disabled on a NON-empty draft, and both survived the pre-fix mutation — proving they encode unchanged behaviour. **§0 fidelity (proposal, not a decision):** the prototype composer (`~/catcode_prototype/cat-app/Chat.jsx:1395-1424`, placeholder `Chat.jsx:1145`) models **no** disconnected/connecting state at all — it is always editable and its only disabled affordance is `disabled={!input.trim()}` on send. The removed strings were therefore desktop **inventions**, so this change moves TOWARD parity, and the ledger rows L479/L480 are updated. Residual: `Connecting…` still shows on the terminal statuses (dead/failed/exited/disconnected), which is a pre-existing lie this change deliberately did not widen — proposed as a separate copy fix. **Supersedes** the M3 clause of `docs/migration/specs/2026-07-14-instant-session-open-design.md:133` ("composer … stays readOnly … quiet 'connecting…' hint"), the same way CC-5 superseded that spec's 300 ms dwell; the dated spec is historical and was not rewritten. **Evidence:** red-before-green by bounded mutation + byte-identical restore (sha256 verified) — reverting the three gate bindings + the placeholder cascade fails 3 `App.test.tsx` tests; forcing `connectPending = false` fails 6 `composerState.test.ts` tests; dropping ONE of the three `releasePendingSubmit` calls fails the wiring tripwire. **Proof layer: helper + SSR-render; the DOM/focus/keystroke and effect-drain layers are UNVERIFIED** — `app/renderer` has no happy-dom (adding one needs sign-off), so no test here can press a key or run a React effect. **Battery:** `bun test app/` 1483/0 (baseline 1456/0, +27) · `bun run --cwd app typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` green (5,546 upstream ignored, 0 new owned) · `test:hardening` 19/19 · `renderer:build` ok. Sidecar count 0→0 (the hardening smoke's own sidecar was reaped). **PENDING operator GUI:** open a not-live session from the sidebar, type immediately — the first keystroke must land with no `Focus to reconnect…`/`Connecting…` and no dropped characters; press Enter within the connect window and confirm the turn starts once ready; then confirm a mid-turn composer still refuses input. ⚠ **That last step and the "Mid-turn is untouched and pinned" claim above are SUPERSEDED by CC-26 2026-08-02:** the guards described there were rewritten because the behavior they pinned was wrong. A mid-turn composer now accepts input and queues the submit; skip that step. |
| **CC-15 · Welcome-launcher recents carried the same basename collision the sidebar just fixed** → `app/renderer/src/sessionsCatalogState.ts` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (local `migration`, not pushed) · headless-only · ⬜ operator GUI acceptance PENDING | **The follow-up CC-14 deliberately left.** `selectRecentWorkspaces` keys the launcher's recent-projects list by `row.cwd` (correct) but labelled every entry `name: basename(row.cwd) || row.cwd` (pre-fix `sessionsCatalogState.ts:583`) — the identical defect, on the surface where the operator PICKS a project to open rather than one where they pick among rows they already recognise, and where each entry carries a per-path `trusted` badge joined from `workspace-trust.snapshot` (`App.tsx:883-892`), so two same-named entries could offer *different* trust decisions under one name (the CC-9 trust-scope confusion class). Latent only because `limit = 6` caps the list. **Fix — the helper was REUSED, not adapted and not forked:** `disambiguateWorkspaceLabels` (`sessionsCatalogState.ts:449-484`, module-private, same file) already takes `readonly string[]` → `Map<cwd, label>` and needed **zero** change; `groupByWorkspace` is byte-identical and its 5 CC-14 tests are untouched and still pass. Depth 1 of the helper *is* `basename(cwd) \|\| cwd`, so an uncontested recent's label is unchanged by construction. **Decision — disambiguate AFTER the cap, over exactly the ≤6 entries that render.** The helper's contract is "shortest label separating it from the others ON SCREEN", and `groupByWorkspace` hands it precisely the set it returns; matching that keeps ONE invariant for both call sites. A 7th, never-rendered `/other/app` must not widen a visible label to resolve a collision the operator cannot see, and because the capped set is a subset the after-cap labels are always equal-or-shorter, never more ambiguous. **This is pinned executably, not by comment:** re-ordering the two statements to disambiguate before the cap makes `47 pass → 46 pass / 1 fail` (`Expected "app", Received "cat-code/app"`); production restored byte-for-byte (md5 `0342ff81…`). **Preserved verbatim:** cwd keying, newest-first `modifiedAtMs` sort, the `limit` slice, `appSessionId`/`live` openable-adoption, `sessionCount` accumulation, the `trusted` join, and the empty-cwd (MAJOR-1) label — asserted alongside the new label claims, not assumed. **Read-time only — no new field, no protocol/sidecar/preload/inbound-vocabulary change** (every cwd is already in hand). **⚠ Premise correction — where the label is actually read.** The dispatch brief assumed the dropdown ROWS render `name`; they do not. `RecentItem` renders the full `recent.cwd` (`WelcomeScreen.tsx:296`, prototype-parity "folder icon + mono path", ledger `:1988`), so the untrusted badge sits beside a full path today and was never ambiguous there. `RecentWorkspace.name`'s **only** current consumer is the collapsed picker TRIGGER (`WelcomeScreen.tsx:219` `recents[0]!.name`) — which is exactly where "which `app` am I in?" is unanswerable, and which now reads `cat-code/app`. The fix is therefore correct and useful, but its live blast radius is one label, and `name` is now right for any future consumer. **➕ Flagged, NOT fixed (needs an owner):** (a) a recent with an empty cwd yields `name: ''`, so the trigger renders a blank button — pre-existing, orthogonal to collisions, and giving it a "Unknown workspace"-style string is a new user-visible string the prototype does not have, so it was not invented here; (b) whether the dropdown rows should switch from full cwd to the disambiguated `name` is a *parity* question (the prototype pins mono path) and is left to the operator, not silently changed. **Red-before-green (proved by reverting, not asserted):** with the production hunk reverted to `9ee891d` via `git show` while the new tests stayed, all 5 new tests fail (`42 pass / 5 fail`) — the operator's collision returns `Received: "app"` for both entries, the different-trust case returns `Received length: 2` for "entries answering to the untrusted label", and the trailing-slash case returns `"app"`; restored byte-for-byte (md5 verified) → `47 pass / 0 fail`. **Coverage:** operator's real two-way collision · uncontested basename stays bare while a neighbour widens · collision with different `trusted` + openable-id adoption riding along · cap boundary in both directions (twin cut ⇒ survivor stays bare; cap raised ⇒ both widen) · degenerate cwds (trailing slash still collides, `basename('/') === ''` still renders `/`, empty cwd unchanged). All five go through the REAL `selectMergedSessionRows` merge — the production feed at `App.tsx:894` — not hand-built rows. **Battery (re-measured 2026-07-26 on a shared dirty tree; baseline `bun test app/` 1451/0 before the change):** `bun test app/` **1456 pass / 0 fail** (132 files, +5) · `bun run --cwd app typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` passed (5546 upstream ignored, **0 owned**) · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `renderer:build` ✓ (588 modules) · `git diff --check` clean. One sidecar I spawned (pid 7004) SIGTERM'd per the CC-3 kill-what-you-spawn rule; the other PPID-1 orphan predates this session and was left alone. **Proof layer: helper/selector (pure read-time projection).** DOM and GUI **UNVERIFIED** — the `app/` renderer suite is SSR-only, so the trigger label was not observed rendered. Operator GUI step: launch with two projects sharing a basename in the six most-recent workspaces, open the Welcome launcher, and read the collapsed Project trigger — it must show `cat-code/app`, not `app`. Ledger row updated (`PARITY-LEDGER.md:1986`, ProjectValue trigger button). |
| **CC-14 · Two sidebar workspace groups both labelled `APP`, adjacent and indistinguishable** → `app/renderer/src/sessionsCatalogState.ts`, `app/renderer/src/Sidebar.tsx` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (`9ee891d`, local `migration`, not pushed) · ✅ **operator GUI-verified 2026-07-26** — sidebar rendered `CAT-CODE/APP` + `PTCLOVE/APP` in place of two identical `APP` headings, with non-colliding groups (`CAT-CODE`, `DISCORDBOT`, `TMP`) left bare (operator: *"looking good"*) | **Bug (operator-reported):** the rail showed two stacked `APP` groups — `/Users/pt/cat-code/app` (12 transcripts) and `/Users/pt/PTClove/app` (1) — with nothing to tell them apart; the operator nearly deleted the wrong one. **Root cause, re-verified in source:** `groupByWorkspace` groups on `row.cwd` (correct — the full path IS the workspace identity, so those genuinely are two groups) but labelled each `name: cwd ? basename(cwd) || cwd : 'Unknown workspace'` (pre-fix `sessionsCatalogState.ts:444`), discarding everything that distinguished the two paths, and then sorted `a.name.localeCompare(b.name)` (`:448`) so the colliding labels landed adjacent — ambiguous became confusing. **Fix — disambiguate only when ambiguous** (the VS Code/JetBrains idiom): a basename nobody contests stays bare (`discordbot`), a colliding one gets the SHORTEST leading path that separates it (`cat-code/app` vs `PTClove/app`). Widening is **progressive, not a fixed one parent segment** — `/a/x/app` and `/b/x/app` both end `x/app`, so a one-segment rule would reproduce the exact defect; each round widens only the still-colliding labels (`disambiguateWorkspaceLabels`, `sessionsCatalogState.ts:469-506`). Exhausting a path falls back to the whole cwd, which is unique by construction *because the cwd is the group key* — that is what bounds the loop. Degenerate cases pinned by test: trailing slash (`/…/app/` still collides with `/…/app`), `basename('/') === ''` still renders `/` not a blank header, empty cwd stays in the `Unknown workspace` bucket and is held OUT of disambiguation (no path to widen). **Read-time only — no new field, no protocol/sidecar/preload change, no inbound vocabulary** (`groupByWorkspace` already receives every cwd). **Ordering:** now sorts on the DISAMBIGUATED label, so what the operator reads stays alphabetical; it remains a pure function of the cwd set, so the FROZEN-order ruling (operator 2026-07-21 — opening a session must never float its workspace) is intact and is asserted directly (order identical for `activeCwd` null / each of 3 cwds). `.localeCompare` gains a `cwd` tiebreak for a total order. **Both consumers fixed by construction** — `Sidebar.tsx:426` and `SessionsPage.tsx:236` both render `group.name`, so zero component change was needed; `rg` found no third consumer. **Prototype gives no answer and none was invented:** `~/catcode_prototype/cat-app/Sidebar.jsx:26` groups on `s.workspace`, a mock STRING label with no path in its data model, so a basename collision is structurally impossible in its fixtures — the documented "prototype mock data is never the contract" case (CLAUDE.md §6). Recorded in the code doc-comment and in 4 `PARITY-LEDGER.md` rows (`:224`, `:225`, `:1187`, `:1189`) so it is not re-litigated. **Red-before-green (proved by reverting, not asserted):** with the production hunk reverted byte-for-byte, all 5 new tests fail (`37 pass / 5 fail`) — the collision case returns `Received: "app"` for both groups and the frozen-order case returns `["app","app","zeta"]`; restored (sha-verified `075f794c…`) → `42 pass / 0 fail`. **Battery (re-measured 2026-07-26 on a shared dirty tree):** `bun test app/` **1451 pass / 0 fail** (132 files) · `bun run --cwd app typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` passed (5546 upstream ignored, 0 owned) · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `renderer:build` ✓ · `git diff --check` clean. **⬜ Residual (flagged, not fixed — out of scope):** `selectRecentWorkspaces` (`sessionsCatalogState.ts:562`) labels the Welcome launcher's recents with the same bare `basename(row.cwd) || row.cwd` and carries the identical latent ambiguity; it is a different selector on a different surface — proposed owner P4-17 follow-on. Two stale `Sidebar.tsx` comments still claiming "current-workspace-first" corrected in passing. |
| **CC-13 · Settings → Permissions was stuck on "Waiting for the engine's permission context…" forever, and its "Default mode" heading showed a session's live mode** → `app/renderer/src/PermissionRulesEditor.tsx`, `app/renderer/src/SettingsShell.tsx`, `app/renderer/src/settingsState.ts`, `app/shared/protocol.ts`, `app/sidecar/settingsDomain.ts` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (local `migration`, not pushed) · ⬜ operator GUI acceptance PENDING | **Bug (operator-reported, re-verified in source):** with no session attached, the whole Permissions pane was one never-resolving line. `PermissionRulesEditor.tsx:35-41` early-returned "Waiting for the engine's permission context…" whenever `context === null`; `SettingsShell.tsx:349-364` fed it ONLY `permissionContext`, which `App.tsx:2304` derives as `selectPermissionContext(permissions, activeSessionId)` — and `activeSessionId` initialises to `null` (`App.tsx:365`), so the selector returns null (`permissionState.ts:303-309`) and nothing ever arrives. **Second, independent defect:** the section headed "Default mode" rendered `context.mode` — the session's live RESOLVED mode, not any default. It looks right on a cold launch (the session usually opens at the default) and silently diverges the moment the mode is changed from the composer. **The real setting was unreachable from the desktop app entirely:** `permissions.defaultMode` (`src/utils/settings/types.ts:59`) had no surface. **⚠ One premise in the dispatch brief was WRONG and is corrected here:** the brief named `snapshot: SettingsSnapshot | null` (`SettingsShell.tsx:165`) as "the correct seam… four lines above the wrong one", implying it is session-independent. It is **not** — `App.tsx:2315` keys it on the same `activeSessionId`, `settingsState.ts:63-69` returns null for a null id, and the frame is emitted per-sidecar at attach *after* the C3 context (`sidecarServer.ts:509-517`). The settings seam is therefore strictly **less** available than the permission seam, never more; switching source alone could not have fixed the reported symptom. What actually fixes it is removing the all-or-nothing gate, which is orthogonal to which seam feeds the value. **Judgement call on the rules half — SPLIT, and the split lands almost entirely on the mode side.** The rules pane's session-scoped sourcing is **correct and stays**: the C3 context is a strict SUPERSET of the settings files that no settings-file read can reproduce — runtime `cliArg`/`command` rules, C1 always-allow suggestions applied mid-turn, PermissionRequest hooks applying rules, auto-mode dangerous-rule stripping (`sidecar/sessionController.ts` post-steps), and above all `managedRulesOnly` (`permissionDomain.ts:35` → `shouldAllowManagedPermissionRulesOnly()`), which can make user/project/local/CLI rules **not load at all** — so rendering settings-file rules there would actively misreport what is enforced. `ruleMetadata` (P4-34) is likewise derived at the sidecar with the engine's own rule parser (`protocol.ts:562-572`) precisely so the renderer never learns the grammar, and `permissionClassifierEnabled` is the runtime gate alone, not a setting (it was a build-time feature gate ∧ runtime gate until CC-21 found the macro always reads false in the unbundled sidecar). Only `permissions.defaultMode` is a persisted global, and only it moved. **Landed:** additive read-only `SettingsSnapshot.permissionDefaultMode = { value, source }` on the EXISTING `settings.snapshot` outbound frame (the CC-9 `trustRoot` precedent) — no new frame kind, no preload channel, no write verb, no version bump. Deliberately NOT added to `EDITABLE_SETTINGS`: that list gates the sidecar write path, and a permission mode must stay read-only (T6b; `permission.setMode` has no `defaultMode` destination, PERMISSION-BOUNDARY.md §3, `permissionDomain.ts:44-48`), with a test asserting the write is refused. **Nested-key precedence handled explicitly:** the engine deep-merges settings (`mergeWith(..., settingsMergeCustomizer)`, `settings.ts:848`), so `defaultMode` resolves on its OWN axis, not on the builder's top-level `seen` set — a higher layer setting only `permissions.allow` must not shadow a lower layer's `defaultMode`, and a test pins exactly that. **Pane restructured:** "Default mode" now shows the SETTING + a `SourceBadge`, or an explicit "not set" (never a borrowed session value); the live mode moved under a "This session" heading keeping its `Current permission mode: <mode>` aria-label; the session-derived sections render only with a context, and their absence is a **terminal statement** ("No session is attached…"), never a wait. **P4-34 Lane 2 is now reachable** — match-type labels, managed-policy state and classifier state were behind this gate and have never been seen live; they still require a session, but the pane no longer blocks the route to them. **Failing-then-passing:** 9 new tests, all 9 fail against HEAD production code restored via `git show` (e.g. *Expected to contain "Default permission mode: plan"*), all 9 pass after; the 11 pre-existing tests in those two files pass in BOTH states; production files restored byte-for-byte (md5 verified). Live-path proof included: a real `settings.json` read through the engine's own `getSettingsForSource` inside `createSidecarSettingsDomain`, not a fixture. **Battery:** `bun test app/` **1446 pass / 0 fail** (baseline re-measured 1433 before the change; +13) · `typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` 0 owned (5546 upstream ignored) · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `renderer:build` ok. **⬜ Residual, stated precisely — the part the constraints forbid closing:** with **zero** sessions ever attached this window still has no engine data at all, so the Default mode section reads "not set" rather than the true persisted value. That is a program-level property of the N-process model (settings reach the renderer only via a sidecar), not of this pane — the General/Managed/Agents panes are equally empty then. Closing it needs a non-session settings read on the host control plane, i.e. a new preload channel, which this task explicitly forbade; **stopped and reported rather than built**. Ledger rows updated (`PARITY-LEDGER.md` §Permissions: the "Waiting…" ux-state row replaced, new default-mode data-binding row added). |

Realized parity **36%** (built+adapted ÷ in-scope; gate target ~80%). ❓ concentrates in two unowned layers the Phase-4 backlog never reaches: **transcript/tool-render `Messages.jsx` (49 — incl. user turns dropped at `TranscriptView.tsx:61`)** + **Settings core value-editors (40 — P4-12 owns only extension panels)**; then Chat activity/streaming (15), Permissions AskUserQuestion (7). **Follow-ons DONE 2026-07-07:** created **P4-18** (transcript rendering, owns 64 ❓) + **P4-19** (settings core editors, owns 45 ❓) as Tranche E; scattered ❓ assigned (P4-0 +3, CC-2 +1) or waived (5 mock/cosmetic); **AskUserQuestion (8) → recommended P4-20, not yet drafted** (operator greenlight — a real reachable tool that today degrades to raw JSON). This also removes a latent unbounded-period bug: anchoring on completion let the period grow to `SESSIONS_CATALOG_WORKER_TIMEOUT_MS` (a 5.5-minute period for a hung run), silently degrading the CATALOG-OWNERSHIP §4 "one interval + one boot" contract.
