| **CC-6 · Browsing history no longer evicts restorable sessions: registry bound 32 → 256 + HC4 cap split** → `app/host/registry.ts`, `app/shared/hostApi.ts`, `app/host/host.ts`, `app/main/devHarness.ts` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (merged `1178d0a` → pushed `ba23218`) | **Closes P4-28 operator findings (2) + (3)** (that row untouched — this is the owning entry). **Bug:** opening a session from history mints a registry row; at the bound `enforceBound` evicts the oldest terminal row, which is a genuinely-restorable recently-closed session — so a **read** action destroyed state, with nothing signalling it (the 2026-07-20 bare-rows ruling removed status chips). Operator's live `registry.json` sat at 32/32 (1 live, 31 closed) on 2026-07-21: permanently at the cap, evicting on every open. **Operator ruling (verbatim scope): raise the cap; protect-restorable / ephemeral-browse-rows / recency-instead-of-count were considered and NOT chosen.** **Landed:** `MAX_REGISTRY_SESSIONS` **32 → 256** (`registry.ts:72`). **Load-bearing finding — the constant was doing double duty:** `host.ts:640` used it as the HC4 **live-process** cap (`liveCount() >= MAX_REGISTRY_SESSIONS`), so raising the row bound would have silently raised the fork-bomb limit to 256 concurrent engine processes (~223 MB each per CC-5's measurement). Split into a new `MAX_LIVE_SESSIONS = 32` in `app/shared/hostApi.ts` beside the existing HC4 rate cap — **effective live limit unchanged**; security baseline preserved, not weakened. Same silent-widening avoided in `devHarness.ts:215`, whose debug-snapshot parse cap was `MAX_REGISTRY_SESSIONS * 4` and would have gone 128 → 1024; pinned to a literal 128. **Measurement (the ruling asked for evidence, not a guess)** — real module driven at N rows, rows shaped from the operator's live registry (~300 B/row), every timed write asserted to have reached disk: 32 rows = 10 KB / `launch()` 0.53 ms / write 0.39 ms; **256 = 98 KB / 1.20 ms / 0.50 ms**; 1024 = 397 KB / 2.87 ms / 0.81 ms; 4096 = 1.6 MB / 10.2 ms / 2.21 ms. So the old A5 "accretion hygiene" justification does not survive contact — A5 is handled independently by the immediate reap of rows that never ran a turn — and **no perf argument distinguishes any value below ~1024**; the measurement fixes only the affordability ceiling, the value itself is a product choice. **Invariants preserved:** live rows still never reaped; the `'parked'` IDLE-PARK exclusion untouched (`registry.ts:578`). **Failing-then-passing test:** `registry.test.ts` "opening a session with 32 restorable rows evicts nothing (a READ must not destroy state)" — at the old bound it fails on the behavioral assertion (`expect(reaped).toEqual([])` → `["app-r-0"]`, a restorable row destroyed), passes after; plus `host.test.ts` "the live-process cap is independent of the registry row bound" as a re-fusion tripwire. **Honest test fixes (not loosened):** `host.test.ts` F5 bound-reap hardcoded `32` and silently stopped exercising the reap once the bound moved → re-seeded off the constant; the F5 failure was caught by the battery, not assumed away. **Battery:** `bun test app/` **1343 pass / 0 fail** (measured baseline 1341/0 on the same tree — the worktree needed `bun install`; a stale-deps run showing 32 fails was environmental, not code) · `typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` 0 owned (5548 upstream ignored) · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `renderer:build` ok · `maps:lint` passed · `git diff --check` clean. **Docs corrected in place:** `decisions/REGISTRY.md` §3 (new value + measurement table + the withdrawn A5/latency rationale), §6.1 + HC4, A5; `decisions/SECURITY-MINIMUM.md` HC4 (now names `MAX_LIVE_SESSIONS`); `decisions/IDLE-PARK.md` line-anchor re-sync forced by my +15-line edit (`:85/:491/:570-609/:578/:777-786/:961-969`, `host.ts:636,664-666`). **⬜ Known residual, accepted + documented:** a *count* cap cannot separate browsing from keeping, so at 256 opened-and-closed sessions eviction resumes; eliminating the class needs one of the three shapes the operator rejected. **⬜ No GUI acceptance run** (headless only; the observable is that browsing many history rows no longer drops restorable ones from the sidebar). |
| **CC-7 · Engine-injected turns render as the operator's own messages: surface `MessageOrigin` over the wire** → `src/utils/messages/mappers.ts`, `src/QueryEngine.ts`, `src/entrypoints/sdk/coreSchemas.ts`, `app/shared/protocol.ts`, `app/renderer/src/transcriptProjector.ts`, `TranscriptView.tsx` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (merged `ba23218`, pushed) · ⬜ operator GUI acceptance PENDING | **Bug (operator-visible):** messages the user never wrote render as their own pink right-aligned bubbles. `MessageOrigin` (`src/types/message.ts:10-41`) has **six** kinds; five are engine-injected but carry `role:'user'`. The desktop handled only `task-notification` (2026-07-22 bug-sweep #4) and `<command-message>` echoes (`transcriptProjector.ts:1593-1595`) — **`coordinator`, `channel`, `teammate`, `deferred-continuation` all fell through to `{kind:'user-text', role:'user'}`** and rendered `flex justify-end` (`TranscriptView.tsx:1132-1138`). **Root cause:** `origin` never crossed the wire (absent from BOTH `engine-types.snapshot.d.ts` and `sdk-types.snapshot.d.ts`), so the projector could only sniff message TEXT — `isTaskNotificationBanner` (`transcriptProjector.ts:1629`) was a hand-copied mirror of the engine's `isTaskNotificationText`, with a doc-comment admitting the copy (repo mistake #10). **Operator ruling: send the field over the wire; per-kind text matchers were considered and REJECTED** (four more engine-logic copies that break silently on wording changes). **Landed:** additive optional `SDKUserMessage.origin?: SDKMessageOrigin` — the frame already carried the message whole, so **no `PROTOCOL_VERSION` bump**; absent still means "the operator typed this", correct for every pre-field emitter (precedent: `tool_use_result`, `mappers.ts:139-145`). Outbound-only: no inbound frame kind, no preload channel; `SDKUserMessageSchema`/`StdinMessageSchema` have no runtime `.parse()` consumers (grep-verified) so widening them adds zero inbound surface — the change *removes* the renderer's need to infer provenance from attacker-influenceable text. **Narrowed at the engine, not forwarded whole:** `channel.meta` is DROPPED — its keys come from a third-party MCP server and `scanForSecrets` matches on key *names*, so a key named `authorization` would fail the guard and cost the entire frame (the same keys-as-values trap that makes `SettingsSnapshot.resolved` an array); `teammate` keeps only the handle, `task-notification` only `status`/`summary`. Two `secretGuard.test.ts` tests assert both halves (what ships passes; the un-narrowed version would not). **Live-path correction — the brief's assumption was wrong:** mid-turn-drained origin messages get `isMeta:true`→`isSynthetic` and the projector already drops them; the REAL leak is `QueryEngine.ts:961-978` yielding `queued_command` attachments as `SDKUserMessageReplay` with no `isSynthetic` and no `origin`. Both that and history replay (`toSDKMessages`) fixed. **Real-store probe before deleting the text mirror** (160 transcripts / 372 injected turns): **0 structured banners without `origin`, 372 with, all 372 non-meta i.e. visible** — that count is the bug's actual blast radius, and deleting the structured-banner sniff regresses nothing measured. Only the frozen `<task-notification>` XML check kept for legacy transcripts. **Latent bug closed en route:** `src/utils/messages.ts` called `taskNotificationOriginFromText` with **no import and no local definition** — a `ReferenceError` on the fallback path (one of two root-tsc errors that disappeared). **Three exhaustiveness tripwires proven by deliberate removal + restore** (projector switch → `TS2322 … not assignable to 'never'`; fixture mapped type → `TS2741`; new engine tripwire in `toSDKMessageOrigin`). **Failing-then-passing:** simulating the pre-fix wire fails **9** projector tests with the exact bug signature (`kind:"user-text"` where `injected-turn` expected). **§0 flags:** `coordinator` + `deferred-continuation` have **no TUI precedent** (isMeta-hidden / rendered as human turns — the latter IS this bug), headings taken from the engine's own `wrapCommandText` → `🔁 adapted`; teammate handle rides neutral accent because Tailwind cannot take an interpolated per-teammate color (the `AGENT_DOT_CLASS` dynamic-class trap) → `🔁 adapted`. **⬜ UNVERIFIED LIVE:** only `task-notification` exists in the operator's store; `coordinator`/`channel`/`teammate`/`deferred-continuation` need an orchestrator/swarm or channel session — headless per-kind coverage only. `2026-07-21-phase4.5-bug-sweep.md` §Bug 4 got a dated SUPERSEDED note (its wire claim is now false) rather than a rewrite. |
| **CC-8 · Terminal `/rename` never reaches the desktop (permanently, once a row is opened)** → `app/renderer/src/sessionsCatalogState.ts`, `app/host/registry.ts`, `app/shared/protocol.ts`, `app/shared/hostApi.ts`, `app/sidecar/sessionsCatalogDomain.ts` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (merged `507be2f`, pushed) · ⬜ operator GUI acceptance **DECLINED 2026-07-26** (operator: *"i wont check it causme im too lazy. skip to next check"*) — the terminal-`/rename`-round-trip check was offered and passed over; not verified, not waived-as-correct | **Bug:** renaming a session from the terminal never shows in the desktop. **Two writers, neither sees the other:** terminal `/rename` writes the engine transcript ONLY (`src/commands/rename/rename.ts:57` → `saveCustomTitle`), never the host registry; `pickTitle` (`sessionsCatalogState.ts:250-254`) returned `registryTitle` whenever non-empty; and the `session-title` frame is *deliberately* not part of attach/replay (`sidecarServer.ts:2484`), so nothing ever re-read the transcript name. **Narrowing that survived the fix — a pure-history row was never broken** (no registry title → the cascade's `customTitle` wins), so only rows **opened in the app** were stuck; the trigger that gives a row a registry title is `openHistorySession.ts:117` seeding it from the catalog on open-from-history — i.e. the far side of bug-sweep Bug 2, whose tab-label fix is what now blocks renames. **Precedence rule chosen: newest *recorded* intent wins** — transcript title wins iff `entry.transcriptTitle` non-empty AND `catalog.capturedAtMs > (descriptor.titleUpdatedAt ?? 0)`, else registry title, else the catalog display cascade. **No existing timestamp could serve** (checked first, not assumed): the `custom-title` JSONL entry carries none (`sessionStorage.ts:2964-2976`), and `modifiedAtMs` is not a proxy because `enrichLog` overrides file mtime with the last in-file `timestamp` (`sessionStorage.ts:5550-5554`) and a title append has none — renaming an idle session does not move it. So two additive signals, both minimal: `SessionsCatalogSnapshot.capturedAtMs`, stamped at the **start** of enumeration so "captured after the registry stamp" *provably* implies the read saw the newer title (stamping at the end would let a pre-rename run win); and `RegistrySession`/`SessionDescriptor.titleUpdatedAt`, additive control-plane field following the `lastMessageSentAt` precedent (`hostApi.ts:87-95`). **Two decisions carry the correctness:** (1) `transcriptTitle` is a NEW field, not the existing `title` — `resolveEntryTitle` is a cascade (`customTitle > summary > firstPrompt > basename`), so letting *that* outrank the registry would replace a real app title with prompt text the moment the `ai-title` entry scrolled out of the bounded head/tail windows; (2) `titleUpdatedAt` stamps **only on genuine change** — restore replays a row's own title through `upsertOnSpawn` (`host.ts:332`), and re-stamping there would let an untouched title outrank a newer rename on every reopen. Desktop renames still win because they write both sides and stamp. **Failing-then-passing:** disabling the new `pickTitle` branch fails 3 tests; 4 companions (desktop rename not reverted, cascade cannot outrank, unknown-age snapshot never outranks, history row still works) pass in BOTH states — they pin what must not change. **Scope note (asked and answered):** 32 files / +694 = **9 load-bearing** (+231/−15), **6 test** (+437), and **17 files of pure fixture churn** (+26 total, one-to-five lines of `titleUpdatedAt: null`) — the tail exists because both new fields are **required** on the shared types, matching house precedent (`lastMessageSentAt`, `cwdExists` are both required-with-explicit-default) rather than optional; deliberate, and reversible for a smaller footprint. **⬜ Residuals:** surfacing is bounded by the catalog timer (~30 s), not instant — a push path was judged out of scope for a display fix; the registry title stays **stale on disk** (display corrected at read time, consistent with never mutating stored rows — a durable reconciliation would be a cross-process write from a stale read, the DR-2 trap). Pre-existing `protocol.ts:1929` doc drift corrected in passing. |
| **CC-9 · Desktop trust prompt understated its scope (approving one folder trusts every sibling in the repo)** → `app/sidecar/workspaceTrustDomain.ts`, `app/shared/protocol.ts`, `app/renderer/src/StartupSurfaces.tsx` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (merged `fae69c5`, pushed) · ⬜ operator GUI acceptance PENDING | **Not a migration-introduced bug — a presentation mismatch that IS ours.** Trust is written at `getProjectPathForConfig()` = `findCanonicalGitRoot(getOriginalCwd())` (`src/utils/config.ts:1626-1638`), i.e. **the git root, never the session cwd**; the read checks that key first then walks up (`config.ts:752-778`, `isPathTrusted` `:790-799`). So approving in `repo/packages/foo` writes one key at `repo` and every sibling package reads trusted — in the desktop AND the terminal CLI, which share the global config. Git-root-keyed trust is upstream Claude Code behavior; it bites here only because **D4 ruled the desktop gate is per-session-create**, which *presents* as per-folder while storage is per-repo. **Operator ruling: fix the wording, leave the engine alone** — changing where trust is stored, or keying the app at the exact cwd (two trust models over one shared config file), were considered and REJECTED. **Landed:** new `executor.getTrustRoot()` returning `getProjectPathForConfig()` — deliberately **the same function that keys the write** (`saveCurrentProjectConfig` reads it at `config.ts:1675`), so displayed scope cannot drift from stored scope and the git root is never re-derived in `app/`; read in its own `try/catch` independent of `trusted` (matching how `detectedRepo` is handled); carried as additive `WorkspaceTrustSnapshot.trustRoot` on the **existing** `workspace-trust.snapshot` outbound frame — no new frame, no version bump, no inbound vocabulary. Three cases all covered: root wider than cwd (names the root inline + sibling/CLI consequence), root == cwd, and root **unresolved** (states the uncertainty rather than asserting a narrow scope it cannot prove). **⚠ Process note — the first round shipped an unrequested visual and was reverted.** It restructured the single path box into a two-row labelled scope block (`SESSION FOLDER` / divider / `TRUST IS SAVED FOR`) and self-flagged it `🔁 adapted` in the after-report — the same flag-it-and-ship-it pattern that produced the 2026-07-20 sidebar status chips the operator reversed on sight. Reverted to **wording-only** on instruction: the path box is byte-identical to pre-change, net visual delta is **one `<p>`** styled like the gate's existing intro. A tripwire test now enforces it (`trust gate keeps ONE Workspace path box — scope is carried in the copy, not extra rows`), asserting exactly one `<code>` element and the absence of the added labels across all three cases. Root cause of the deviation was an under-specified dispatch prompt (mine), not agent judgment. **P4-25 fail-closed re-verified:** `sidecarServer.ts` byte-identical (`git diff --stat` empty), `-t "P4-25"` passes, plus two new tests locking in that a throwing `getTrustRoot()` yields `trustRoot:null` with `trusted:false` and never discards a known `trusted:true`. **⬜ Residuals:** Settings → Workspace (`WorkspaceTrustSection.tsx`) now has `trustRoot` available but does not render it — left alone deliberately (the ruling scoped this to the consent moment); and the pre-existing engine asymmetry stands, unfixed and out of scope — the domain READS via `isPathTrusted(cwd)` (walks up from session cwd) but WRITES via `getProjectPathForConfig()` (memoized off `getOriginalCwd()`); they coincide only because the sidecar is spawned in the session cwd (P3-1), so a future divergence would write trust where the read never looks. Displaying the true write key makes such a divergence visible rather than silent. |
| **CC-10 · Terminal-created sessions take ~51 s to appear in the sidebar (A2, unparked)** → `app/main/sessionsCatalogRunner.ts` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (merged `f277249`, pushed) · ⬜ operator GUI acceptance PENDING | **Supersedes the CC-5 "A2 PARKED" note.** Operator measured ~51 s against a 30 s target in the 2026-07-22 GUI run. **Both standing theories REFUTED by measurement before any code changed** — this bug already had two refuted hypotheses on record, so a blind fix was explicitly off the table. (1) *"the live run is slower than the hermetic harness"* — **dead**: the real worker runs **0.45 s** end-to-end against the real corpus (60 project dirs / 337 enumerable transcripts / 1.0 GB), split as engine-graph import 326 ms + `initializeSidecarRuntime` 46 ms + `enumerateSessionsCatalog` 288 ms; RAM-0's ~0.9 s was pessimistic. (2) *"the delivery hop is slow"* — **dead**: `onCatalog` → `sendHostEvent` → `contents.send` → `dispatchSessionsCatalog` is synchronous with no batching or throttle (`main.ts:394,714`; `App.tsx:673`), the read path has no cache (fresh `readdir`+`stat` per run), and the transcript writer flushes at 100 ms. (3) A hermetic end-to-end probe measured the app's **entire** contribution at **2.14 / 5.96 / 11.00 s** against a 30.36 s ceiling. **Where the ~20 s actually is — upstream, and structurally invisible to the app:** a transcript file is only created on the **first user/assistant message** (`materializeSessionFile`, `sessionStorage.ts:1290,1319`) and a `hasConversation === false` row is dropped (`sessionsCatalogDomain.ts:181`). Corroborated across 120 real transcripts: `(first user/assistant timestamp − file birthtime)` has **p50 −0.13 s** — the file comes into existence *at* the first message. A stopwatch started at "I created a session in the terminal" therefore counts operator typing + first-turn time before anything exists to enumerate, which also explains the reported *range* (~30–51 s) rather than a fixed number. **Honest boundary:** items 1–3 are measured; the residual attribution is proven negatively (provably not in the app's refresh path) plus positively via the materialization gate, but the operator's stopwatch start cannot be reconstructed. One in-app alternative also brackets 51 s — a **single failed run** cost a full extra interval (~60.7 s pre-fix) and failures reach only stderr; no log survives from 2026-07-22 to distinguish them. **Fix — neither prior recommendation was right as stated** (CC-5's "lengthen the interval" makes it worse; "fixed cadence" is correct but buys 0.36 s, not 20 s). What fixed cadence actually buys is closing a **latent unbounded-period bug**: the next run now anchors to the current run's **start** (`Math.max(0, intervalMs - (now() - startedAt))`) instead of its completion, so the period is exactly `intervalMs` instead of `intervalMs + runDuration`. Completion-anchoring let the period grow to `SESSIONS_CATALOG_WORKER_TIMEOUT_MS` — a **5.5-minute** refresh for a hung run — silently violating the ratified CATALOG-OWNERSHIP §4 "one interval + one boot" contract. Single-flight untouched (next run still only *scheduled* after the current settles; an overrun clamps to delay 0 rather than compounding); the one-shot main-supervised shape is unchanged, no locked decision reopened. **Re-measured after:** observed period between run starts **30.000 / 30.001 / 30.000 / 30.000 s** (was 30.36). **The 30 s interval value was deliberately NOT changed** — operator-owned tuning knob with a RAM tradeoff, mean latency already ~15 s. **Failing-then-passing:** 2 colocated tests fail pre-fix (`30000` vs expected `29550` / `0`); one also asserts `maxConcurrent === 1` so the fix cannot regress single-flight. **⬜ Residuals with numbers:** app-side worst case still ~30.0 s / mean ~15 s, bounded by the interval (cost per run 0.45 s wall / ~340 ms CPU / ~307 MB transient, nothing resident between ticks). **⬜ Open, worth a decision:** a failed run still costs a full extra interval (~60 s) with no retry or backoff, and failures are only visible on stderr — if A2 recurs live that is the first thing to check, and there is currently no way to check it after the fact. |
| **CC-11 · Codex diagnostic appenders mint orphan transcripts (they were LIVE, not latent)** → `src/utils/sessionStorage.ts`, `src/utils/cleanupRegistry.ts` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (merged `d378d25`, pushed) · headless-only, no GUI observable | **⚠ Premise correction — this row exists because the lead got it wrong twice.** The task was dispatched as "close a latent landmine: these recorders have zero call sites." **False.** `recordCodexSendPath` and `recordCodexStreamSurface` (`sessionStorage.ts:465,502`) are invoked from `src/services/api/codex-fetch-adapter.ts:255,1668` and were actively firing. **Why the lead's grep lied — a repo-wide hazard worth knowing:** `codex-fetch-adapter.ts` line ~80 contains a **literal NUL byte** in a string constant (`STICKY_WILDCARD_ACCOUNT`, a deliberate sentinel written as a raw byte rather than an escape), so `file(1)` reports the file as `data` and **`rg` and `git grep` skip it silently** — all 3,634 lines invisible with no warning. Swept 2026-07-26: it is the **only** such file in `src/`. The failure is also *invocation-dependent* (a directory-walk `rg` can surface hits while piping the same file through stdin prints only `binary file matches`), so an apparent hit does not prove completeness either — use `rg -a` for anything load-bearing in that file. Fixing the sentinel to `'\\0any'` is behaviourally identical and would restore searchability; **not done** — another session had uncommitted edits in that exact file. **Severity was higher on this branch than on `main`:** `main` guards the send-path callback with `if (!isPrewarm && onSendPathComplete)`, `migration` has only `if (onSendPathComplete)` — and prewarm sends fire **before any user message**, precisely the orphan-minting window. This is the mechanism behind the 162-of-514 conversation-less transcripts the operator trashed on 2026-07-20. **Landed:** ownership predicate `getOwnedTranscriptPath()` gating on `Project.sessionFile !== null` — **not invented**, it is the same signal the engine already uses for this exact bug class (`sessionStorage.ts:879-881` "prevents metadata-only session files"; `insertMessageChain` `:1349` materializes only on a real user/assistant message; `adoptResumedSessionFile` `:1878` reasons about orphans in its own doc-comment). Three properties fall out free: it writes to `sessionFile` itself rather than re-deriving, so it can never target an unmaterialized path mid-`switchSession`; it inherits `shouldSkipPersistence()` (test env, `--no-session-persistence`); and it reads the module singleton directly so a diagnostic never constructs a `Project` as a side effect. **Extended to `recordPromptCacheBreak` (`:626`, also live via `promptCacheBreakDetection.ts:821`)** — both branches now closed against orphan *creation*; the agent branch uses `getOwnedAgentTranscriptPath()`, grounded in the existing `activeSubagents` registry (`cleanupRegistry.ts:41`, entries carry `transcriptPath`; registered at `AgentTool.tsx:1186` / `resumeAgent.ts:306`). `cleanupRegistry.ts` is **+12/−0, purely additive** (one exported read accessor; `registerCleanup`/`runCleanupFunctions`/`register`/`unregisterActiveSubagent`/`flushStallDetectedEntries` untouched — **no cleanup or shutdown behavior changed**). **Regression caught in its own WIP:** the first agent-branch guard was over-tight — `LocalMainSessionTask.ts:102` links its own agent transcript and passes that `agentId` to the API at `:125` but never calls `registerActiveSubagent`, so a backgrounded task's cache-break diagnostics would have been **silently dropped**; fixed with an existence fallback. Deliberately NOT closed by registering that task in `activeSubagents`, because `flushStallDetectedEntries` (`cleanupRegistry.ts:61`) iterates that map on exit and would emit spurious `stall-detected` records — a real behavior change needing its own ruling. **Failing-then-passing:** reverting the guards fails 2 tests ("no owning session writes nothing", "an unregistered agent writes nothing"); three positive-coverage tests pass in BOTH states, proving the guard costs no legitimate diagnostic coverage. **Battery:** focused suites 149/0 across 7 files · `build:dev:full` green · typecheck delta measured by restore-then-reapply, **zero new** (1895 total / 48 in touched files, identical both states). **⬜ Residual, stated precisely:** on the agent branch the second tier is an *existence proxy*, not an ownership assertion — an unregistered agentId whose derived path already exists will be appended to. It cannot **create** a file, so no orphan is minted, but it could append to a stale transcript under a recycled id. Closing that fully needs every agent-shaped caller in one registry, which is the `LocalMainSessionTask` ruling above. |
| **CC-12 · The sidebar offered sessions that cannot be opened (`resume-failed` on click)** → `app/host/host.ts`, `app/shared/limits.ts` | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-26** (`c7b32a6`, local `migration`, not pushed) · ✅ **operator GUI-verified 2026-07-26** — created a session, typed nothing, closed it: its sidebar row vanished and no in-use session was collaterally dropped (operator: *"yeah it working."*) | **Bug (operator-reported, reproduced from the live registry):** clicking or restarting certain sidebar rows failed with `[sidecar] resume-failed: … no conversation found (transcript missing or unreadable)`. **Mechanism, re-verified in source:** the engine materializes a transcript only on the FIRST user/assistant message (`src/utils/sessionStorage.ts:1354` `materializeSessionFile`, called from `:1387`), but the registry stamps `engineSessionId` from the READY frame i.e. at spawn (`host.ts` `onSupervisorEvent` → `registry.fillEngineSessionId`). So every session opened and never typed in holds a non-null `engineSessionId` pointing at a file that does not exist. The launch reap already drops those (`registry.ts` `transcriptMissing`), but only BETWEEN launches — and `isRestorable` explicitly relied on that ("the launch reap already dropped rows whose transcript is gone"), so a row that acquired its id DURING the run was never re-checked and stayed advertised as restorable for the rest of the run. `restoreSession` did guard (`registry.hasTranscript`, §9-A4); **`restartSession` did not** — and it is the path that bumps `restartCount`, matching the operator's `restartCount: 2` and two identical failures. **Live blast radius: 1 of 26 rows carrying an `engineSessionId` had no transcript anywhere on disk** (measured read-only against `~/.cat-code/desktop/registry.json`); small but self-inflicted — every never-typed session becomes one. **Landed (3 changes + 2 required consequences):** (1) `isRestorable` now consults `registry.hasTranscript` at READ time, so the descriptor finally matches its own declared contract (`hostApi.ts` "Registry row + transcript both present", REGISTRY.md §6.1) — this also covers a transcript pruned mid-run; (2) `restartSession` gained the same guard, closing the asymmetry with `restoreSession`; (3) the exit handler classifies `RESUME_FAILED_EXIT_CODE` and retires the id for the run (in-memory `resumeFailed` set, no registry-schema change) — this is the only signal for the class `existsSync` cannot see: a transcript FILE that exists but carries no loadable conversation, exactly what the sessions catalog already drops as `hasConversation === false` (`sessionsCatalogDomain.ts` — the doctrine matched, not invented). Consequences the tests forced: `listSessions` and `closeSession`/spawn-failure now filter on the descriptor's OWN `restorable` flag rather than a second copy of the predicate — without this a just-closed no-transcript session came BACK as a live-looking tab, because the renderer folds `restorable === false` into TAB membership (`shellState.ts` foldTabMembership). The CRASH path deliberately still emits a status and keeps its tab (restart-in-place is the point). `RESUME_FAILED_EXIT_CODE` moved from `app/sidecar/index.ts` to `app/shared/limits.ts` beside `PARKED_EXIT_CODE`, per that constant's own "defined ONCE so exiter and classifier never drift" doc. **Perf judgement (asked for, measured not guessed):** `hasTranscript` is one `existsSync` per NON-LIVE row, and only `listSessions` fans out. Measured on the real machine: **256 rows = 0.138 ms warm / 0.111 ms all-missing per `listSessions()`**. Hottest caller is `idleParkDriver.evaluate` (each host event + a 60 s timer) → negligible. The one real risk was `canPreview`, which called `listSessions()` and is itself called once per cached file by main's startup `gcTranscriptCache` — O(rows²) ≈ 35 ms of main-thread `existsSync` at 256/256. Mitigated by deriving `canPreview` from THIS id's descriptor (provably equivalent: the union keys on the same flag), not by adding a cache. **Tests (red-before-green, 3 bounded mutations, each restored):** `host.test.ts` — never-typed session is not offered (mutating `isRestorable` → *Expected false, Received true* on the union assertion); `restartSession` refuses a no-transcript resume (mutating the guard → *Expected false, Received true*); `RESUME_FAILED_EXIT_CODE` retires an id whose FILE exists (mutating the classifier → *Expected false, Received true*), with an ordinary crash as the contrast control. Renderer projection proven through the real `reduceShellState`, not asserted on internals. One existing fixture corrected honestly (`F4 restartCount` never wrote a transcript → now does, like its sibling restart test). **Battery:** `bun test app/` **1430 pass / 0 fail** · `typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` 0 owned (5546 upstream ignored) · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `renderer:build` ok · `maps:lint` passed · `git diff --check` clean. **Security:** no new inbound frame, no preload channel, no protocol change — baseline trivially intact. **Stale refs swept:** `IDLE-PARK.md` ×3 (constant location + the now-conditional `isRestorable` claim), `spawnConfig.probe.test.ts` comment. **⬜ No GUI acceptance run** — operator steps: open a session, do NOT type in it, close it → the row must NOT appear as a restore-offer in the sidebar (pre-fix it appeared and failed on click). **⬜ Known residual (pre-existing, not a regression):** a session parked by IDLE-PARK before it ever ran a turn has no transcript, so unpark-on-click is refused — `restoreSession` always refused it; the descriptor is merely honest about it now. |

| **CC-21 · Two-week audit-and-fix sweep of `bcfe0db..5433af8` (237 commits / 538 files / +79.6k)** → 13 read-only auditors, 12 fix agents, exclusive file ownership per agent | ✅ **LANDED 2026-07-28** (local `migration`, not pushed; 11 commits `fdca782`..`26c8875`) · ⬜ **operator GUI acceptance PENDING on the two worst bugs** | Operator asked for an audit because the two-week run shipped without review. ~70 findings; the batteries were green the whole time, which is the point: **every finding below passed CI.** **Worst two, both fixed but UNEXERCISED:** Enter on the permission card's own **Deny** button *allowed* the tool (the global shortcut treated any non-INPUT target as "nobody typing", then `preventDefault()`d the button's own click, `App.tsx:1735-1771`); and with a permission + an `AskUserQuestion` pending together a single Enter resolved BOTH, so answering a question silently approved an unrelated Bash command. **No test in `app/` can press a key** — `App` takes no props, its data arrives only through effects `renderToStaticMarkup` never runs, both `App` and `SessionPane` call hooks so the direct-invocation convention throws, and there is no jsdom/happy-dom in the tree. Both fixes are pinned only by exact-expression source tripwires. **These two need an operator keypress or a DOM harness (harness needs sign-off).** **Other HIGHs closed:** an unusable deferred-continuation store blocked *every prompt in every session* and the advertised recovery threw the same error; a crashed `submitted` job bricked the session permanently (16 of 60 real project dirs on this machine hit the triggering 0755 check); autocompaction made a *successful* continuation report "Stopped, needs you"; CLI `--resume` never armed the provider-switch lock, so a GPT session could be moved onto Anthropic mid-conversation; the session inspector was mounted without `sessionState`, so workspace trust / live permission rules / effective settings / flags / diagnostics rendered **nowhere** after CC-19 deleted them from Settings citing that inspector; one oversized frame **erased the whole replay ring**; the catalog and accounts-pool workers were never killed on quit, orphaning a ~189 MB engine-graph process invisible to every reaper; `remoteSettings.directConnect` accepted any renderer string as a URL (renderer-originated egress + session cwd, defeating `connect-src 'self'`; **predates the window**); writing an overridden setting turned that row into a permanently uneditable `unknown`; ⌘K with the Tasks dialog open killed a running background task. **Test-quality finding worth its own note:** the regression test for the 30-of-32 lost-run-facts outage drove a file the fix never touched and passed on the pre-fix tree; the line actually fixed had zero coverage. **Swept clean (do not re-audit):** em dash across `app/renderer/src` (910 hits, all comments), the Tailwind v4 dynamic-class trap (131 interpolations, 0 traps), feature/migration wiring (4/4), `src/codex-core/` (untouched all window — rotation machinery intact). **Deletions parked, not made:** `WorkspaceTrustSection` / `DiagnosticsSection` / `BannerStack` / `ResolutionOrderLegend` all have zero importers, but `STATUS.md:43`, `backlog/phase4.md:828`, `INVENTORY.md:90` and the 2026-07-21 cut-list audit treat them as live work — and `settingsScope.ts:710-716` says the replacement inspector was still awaiting its wiring, so the data rendered in **neither** place. Deleting would have destroyed the only implementation. **Battery:** `bun test app/` **1769 pass / 0 fail** (baseline 1711) · `app typecheck` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` clean (5545 upstream ignored) · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `build:dev:full` green · root typecheck 1896 (baseline 1897). **⬜ Open:** ~25 findings unfixed, incl. autocompact misclassification, the `mock.module` poisoning (an `afterAll` restore does NOT work — bun installs module mocks at import time; needs DI or a `.probe.` rename), `mainSource.test.ts` executing zero production code, the text guard scanning only `app/renderer/src` despite `2e71168` claiming repo-wide, and `sessionController.ts:206-232` carrying the same settings-beats-env defect in the desktop plane. **WAVE 3 (same session) closed the ~20 residuals** in 7 more commits `10dfbef`..`9b7ef70`. Battery after: `bun test app/` **1799 pass / 0 fail** (148 files, from 1711/142 at the sweep's start) · app typecheck clean · sidecar clean · hardening 19/19 · `build:dev:full` green · root typecheck 1896. **Two results worth reading even if nothing else here is:** (1) the old `app/main/mainSource.test.ts` was proven worthless by mutation — with the renderer bridge changed to never translate an event, single-use cwd tokens made infinitely reusable, and backfill reading the oldest rows instead of the newest, **all 18 of its tests still passed**; the replacement Electron-free module (`app/main/mainDecisions.ts`) fails 4 tests on those same three mutations. (2) the deferred-continuation suites reported 31 pass / **16 fail** when run together while each file was green alone, because bun installs `mock.module` at IMPORT time for the whole invocation — **an `afterAll` restore cannot work** (measured, twice). Fixed by injecting the durable writers through `QueryEngineConfig`; those five files are now **56/0** together. Three further leaking suites were found and deliberately NOT fixed (`Settings/Usage.test.tsx`, `Settings/Settings.test.tsx`, `codex-core/accounts.test.ts` — 10-13 unconditional module mocks each, and the last is the §6-fenced cross-process token file). **The `feature()` divergence is now quantified and is the biggest open item:** 911 call sites / 87 flags in `src/` all evaluate FALSE in the sidecar because it is spawned unbundled; `./cli-dev` enables 37 of those names covering 463 call sites, **436 of them outside the TUI**. Three behavioural divergences named: the desktop parses Bash with a DIFFERENT parser and Bash permission analysis is built on that parse (security-relevant); built-in Explore/Plan subagents exist in the terminal and not in the app; memory extraction never runs in the desktop. This is already on record as **ruling #1, open since 2026-07-21** while `app/` shipped surfaces on top of it. Needs an operator decision (bundle the sidecar / pass an explicit manifest at spawn / ratify the empty set), not a patch. **Text guard now covers `main`/`host`/`sidecar`, not just the renderer**, and names 11 real violations with owners; the worst is `agentConfigDomain.ts:22`, which trips all three §7 clauses at once. **⬜ Still open after wave 3:** the three leaking suites above · `AgentConfigSnapshot.availableMcpServers` and `MemorySnapshot.notes` (3rd and 4th unread wire fields; the latter now ships `notes: []` purely to satisfy the contract) · a user frame carrying ONLY a tool_result is still not marked seen, so its redelivery still churns · `PARITY-LEDGER.md:1680` claims the Memory Scope notes are rendered (they were deleted) · `docs/maps/web-app-runtime.md:67,69` cite the now-deleted `mainSource.test.ts` (that file is another session's uncommitted work, so it was left alone). **⬜ UNCHANGED AND STILL THE POINT: the two worst bugs remain fixed-but-unexercised.** No test in `app/` can press a key. Operator check: Bash permission pending → Tab to Deny → Enter must deny. Full triage: `scratchpad/TRIAGE.md` + `scratchpad/WAVE3.md` (this session). |

## PHASE 3 — shell + multi-session build-out
Gate: two real sessions in one window, each its own engine process, switchable, app-owned registry — plus the D6 §4 lifetime line (quit/relaunch restores both sessions from the registry, crash-sim reaps orphans + still offers restore, proven **anti-Potemkin**).

**✅ Phase-3 GATE CLEARED 2026-07-06** — P3-8 passed live (agent-driven cua-driver GUI + on-disk anti-Potemkin proof): clean-quit→relaunch→restore, restart-in-place (LR-1), and SIGKILL-host crash-sim → orphan reap → restore, both sessions recalling their pre-quit nonce in fresh engine processes. A restore **tip-selection blocker** (resume loaded a lone trailing `system` frame instead of the conversation) was found and fixed mid-gate + regression-tested; full evidence in the P3-8 row. **Phase-4 backlog is generated next in a separate session.**
→ **BACKLOG GENERATED 2026-07-04** (Scenario 2, from INVENTORY W2 rows + the decided D1/D6/F3 + SECURITY-MINIMUM Addendum, per PROGRAM-PLAN §6/§8). **9 sessions in three tranches:** protocol/host plumbing (P3-0..P3-3) → renderer/shell (P3-4..P3-7) → lifetime gate (P3-8). JSON stayed VALID — the atomic temp+rename write prevents tearing but NOT lost updates: engine-side `persistPermissionUpdates` writes back the full in-memory view without re-read/merge or a cross-process lock. At that point, the engine-side concern was not fixed in P3-5a and was **FLAGGED as a P3-8 rider.** Clearing P3-5a then required the engine-side read-merge/lock fix plus a same-cwd two-writer re-run confirming both rules landed with no loss.
**ENGINE-SIDE FIX LANDED 2026-07-05 (headless-proven, then GUI re-verified).** Root cause was two-part, not one: (1) `updateSettingsForSource` (`src/utils/settings/settings.ts`) had no cross-process lock around its read-merge-write — fixed with a `proper-lockfile` `lockSync` + bounded retry loop (`acquireSettingsLockSync`; `lockSync` itself cannot retry — "Cannot use retries with the sync api" — so the retry loop is required, not cosmetic). (2) The lock alone was proven insufficient (~1/3 of probe runs still lost a rule): callers (`addPermissionRulesToSettings`, `deletePermissionRuleFromSettings`, and the `addDirectories`/`removeRules`/`removeDirectories` cases in `persistPermissionUpdate`) pre-computed the full final array from a **cached, pre-lock** read, and `updateSettingsForSource`'s merge replaces arrays wholesale by design — so a stale pre-lock array still clobbered the other process's just-landed write despite fully serialized file access. Fix: `updateSettingsForSource` now accepts a `SettingsUpdater` function (`current => next | null`) invoked *under the lock* with the fresh on-disk read; all five read-modify-write call sites converted to this form (`setMode`/`replaceRules` correctly left as-is — no concurrent state to preserve). New two-real-process probe (`src/utils/settings/settingsWriteContention.probe.test.ts` + `.probe.child.ts`, house pattern per `accountRefreshContention.probe.test.ts`) calls the real production `persistPermissionUpdate` from two spawned processes racing one `settings.local.json`: **pre-fix ~2/3 runs lost a rule, lock-only ~1/3 still lost a rule, full fix 15/15 clean** (independently re-verified). `tsc --noEmit` zero new errors (diffed against clean HEAD); `bun test src/utils/settings src/utils/permissions` + `bun test app/` (368 pass) show no regressions (4 pre-existing unrelated failures in `enableAutoModeFlag.test.ts`, confirmed identical on clean HEAD). **GUI RE-VERIFICATION PASSED 2026-07-05 (human-driven, two sessions both in `/Users/pt/catcode-gui-scratch`, following the same-cwd two-writer protocol above).** Two fresh, distinguishable Bash commands (`race=A`, `race=B`) sent from two separate tabs at the same cwd, both reaching "Permission required" simultaneously; both approved via the "Always allow" suggestion (not the plain one-time "Allow" button — a driving mistake in an earlier automated attempt this same day caused a false non-result by only exercising one-time allow, never persisting a rule at all). Result read directly off disk: `cat /Users/pt/catcode-gui-scratch/.cat-code/settings.local.json` → both `Bash(curl -s "https://example.com/?race=A")` and `Bash(curl -s "https://example.com/?race=B")` present under `permissions.allow` — no loss. This closes the anti-Potemkin bar: headless probe (15/15) + live GUI same-cwd two-writer run both clean. **P3-5 SPLIT 2026-07-05 (5a of 2; prompt in `backlog/phase3.md`).** Root routing = real projection of the HostEvent stream + P3-4 stores (replaces AppV2 mock glue); establishes the shell's visual grammar 5b extends. | **P3-6** WorkspaceLayout: 1–3 split panels, resize, drag | ANY 🖐 GUI | 5 | ✅ 2026-07-07 | **GUI-VERIFIED 2026-07-07 (agent-driven via cua-driver, AX-cited) — all core mechanics + the relaunch fix PASS.** Panels host any open session; layout is RENDERER-owned persistence (`localStorage` appSessionId refs, registry-unaware), degrades gracefully on reaped sessions; same-session duplicate focuses existing panel rather than mirroring. dep: P3-5. ∥ P3-7/P3-8. **REVIEWED 2026-07-07 (two cold subagent passes, report-only): code-vs-plan ✅ GREEN** — all 7 load-bearing claims verified (renderer-owned/registry-unaware D1 §3 with zero `app/shared`+preload+main+host touches, graceful reap degradation, concurrent per-panel rendering, same-session=focus-not-mirror, 1–3 bound, identity+state aria-labels, 🟡 honesty). **Code review — 1 HIGH + 2 MED + LOWs; blockers FIXED (uncommitted on top of `482579f`, verified green):** HIGH — drop-edge strips overlaid the panel × / selector making close unclickable in every split → `DropEdge` now `pointer-events-none` at rest, `pointer-events-auto` only while a tab drag is in flight (`WorkspacePanels.tsx` `dragActive`), + a markup test asserting the rest state; MED — `reconcileWorkspaceLayout` forced `activeIndex=0` for multi-panel splits lacking the active session → now adopts the preferred session's panel index when shown, else keeps the prior focus; MED — panel `key` included the index → remount-on-reorder dropped scroll/view state → now `key={panel.sessionId}`; dead `onTabDragStart` prop (flagged by BOTH passes — two-strikes) REMOVED. **DEFERRED LOWs (non-blocking, follow-up):** `normalizeWidths` snaps all panels to equal when a split drops a width below the 20% min (#5); tab-click overwrites the focused panel's session in place (#4, likely intentional); one-frame width off-by-one on middle-session removal that self-heals (#6); a redundant `liveSessionIds`/`liveSessionKey` reconcile dep. Post-fix: `bun test app/` **398/0**, renderer tsc clean, sidecar tsc 5548 pre-existing/zero-in-`app/`, hardening 19/19. **GUI ACCEPTANCE 2026-07-07 (cua-driver, AX-cited): core mechanics PASS** — 2 panels streaming concurrently (`Panel 1 … inactive` / `Panel 2 … active`, msg counts grew in both, human performed the tab→edge DnD since cua-driver's synthetic drag doesn't trigger HTML5 DnD); close-button-in-split works (`Close panel 2 showing …` click collapsed 3→1, proves the HIGH fix); divider resize via keyboard (960→832→1017px); 3-panel + 4th-split refused (`Workspace layout supports up to three panels.`); close-session → graceful degrade (3→2, no blank/crash). Same-title "already open" is by-design (duplicate=focus). **ONE GAP FOUND → FIXED (operator-authorized, headless-proven; GUI re-verify of the relaunch check PENDING before ✅):** the saved split did NOT survive a full relaunch — on startup `reconcileWorkspaceLayout` ran against the not-yet-restored (restorable) sessions, dropped their panels, and the write effect re-persisted the degraded 1-panel layout, destroying the save before restore. **First (fragile) fix — REVERTED:** a `preservableSessionIds` set kept restorable panels in the active layout; GUI re-verify showed it made the panel COUNT survive but hid the live startup session behind "No sessions open" (preserved panels render nothing → `workspacePanels.length===0 → EmptyShell`, `App.tsx:731/794`), and clicking that session to escape reassigned a panel → wrong session restored. Order-dependent, not clean. **Proper fix (re-apply-on-restore, renderer-only):** the active layout degrades NORMALLY on relaunch (live startup session stays visible, no hidden EmptyShell); the disk-loaded MULTI-panel split is held in a `pendingRestore` state; the disk write is GATED while pending (so the startup degrade can't overwrite the save); `readyToRestoreLayout` (`workspaceLayout.ts`) snaps the saved split back ATOMICALLY once EVERY referenced session is live again — order-independent, overriding any interim clicks; pending is abandoned if a referenced session is unrecoverable (unblocks the write), and cleared on deliberate split/assign/close (so it can't clobber a new layout). Restore preserves the same `appSessionId` (proven in P3-7 check #3), so the referenced sessions match. Scratch home `/tmp/p3-8-gate-home` (full-copy `.cat-code.json` creds, account `main`). **RUN 1 (clean-quit → relaunch → restore):** A `dfdd2073` (nonce ARDVARK-A, cwd `~/cat-code`) and B `002935de` (BOBCAT-B, `~/cat-code/app`) restored from Sidebar → each answered **its own** nonce live; transcripts appended under NEW engine PIDs; `[sidecar] resume-seeded messages=5` for BOTH. **RUN 2a restart-in-place (LR-1):** tab RESTART on A → re-spawn SAME `engineSessionId dfdd2073`, new PID, `resume-seeded messages=7`, re-answered ARDVARK-A (proves `restartSession` threads the row's engine id). **RUN 2b crash-sim:** SIGKILL host → both gate sidecars SURVIVED as orphans (PPID→1, socket outlives parent = behavior-not-welding, L2); relaunch sweep **reaped the exact orphan PIDs** (`[registry] swept orphaned sidecar pid=49150/47174`), rows marked `crashed` (not clean); both restored → ARDVARK-A / BOBCAT-B, `resume-seeded messages=9/7`, new PIDs. Zero `resume-failed`. Anti-Potemkin (a)+(b)+(c) all hold per session per run. **BLOCKER FOUND & FIXED mid-gate** (this is the anti-Potemkin clause doing its job — a unit-test-invisible defect): first restore answered *"I don't have a magic word"* with `resume-seeded messages=1` (a lone `system` frame, no history). Root cause — the engine resume **tip-selection** picked the newest *non-sidechain* leaf, which is a trailing Codex `system` diagnostic (`codex_send_path` / `account.route.selected`, written `parentUuid:null` with a ms-later timestamp than the assistant turn); `buildConversationChain` from it collapses to 1 message, so a restored session held NO prior context. Fix (engine, surgical): `getLastSessionLog` (`src/utils/sessionStorage.ts:4275`) and its `.jsonl`-path twin `loadMessagesFromJsonlPath` (`src/utils/conversationRecovery.ts:427`) now select the newest **user/assistant** leaf, mirroring the already-correct `loadFullLog` (`:3358`). Missed by `resumeSeed.probe.test.ts` because its `recordTranscript` fixtures have no trailing dangling system frames. Regression test added (`sessionStorage.test.ts` → *"resume tip skips a trailing dangling system frame"*), tripwire-verified (reverting the fix → `firstPrompt:"No prompt"`). `bun test app/` **375 pass**, app tsc clean, sidecar tsc no new errors, hardening **19/19**. **TUI impact VERIFIED 2026-07-06 (headless, real transcripts):** the same bug hit TUI `--resume` of real Codex sessions — a real 1096-row session (`f5f183a0`) resumed with only **3 messages** of context pre-fix vs **203** post-fix (a second, `47a35d19`, 3→5). All TUI resume entry points (`main.tsx:3784` direct-UUID, `resume.tsx:235` `/resume`, plus `--continue`) funnel through the same `getLastSessionLog`/`loadFullLog`/`loadMessagesFromJsonlPath`, so **this commit fixes the daily-driver TUI too** — no separate change needed. |

Strong lead: the sessions catalog is point-in-time, `the transcript enumeration runs once at spawn` (`app/shared/protocol.ts:1924-1928`, verified), so a session absent at enumeration is absent for that sidecar's lifetime. **NOT YET CLOSED:** a session opened FROM history should already be in the catalog, so spawn-freezing explains a stuck app-created row cleanly but not obviously the from-history case; the open unknown is which sidecar's catalog snapshot the merge reads and whether the opened row is present in it. Do not fix from this row until that is closed. **(2) A read action silently evicts restorable state.** Every open-from-history mints a registry row; live rows are never eligible for eviction, so at the cap the rows pushed out are genuinely-restorable recently-closed sessions. `2a6f39d` stopped *unopenable* transcripts from minting rows ("nine failed opens, nine reaps"); a legitimate open still takes a slot. Cost is unobservable by design — the 07-20 bare-rows ruling removed status chips, so nothing signals that browsing just demoted a session. **Live state at report time: `~/.cat-code/desktop/registry.json` = 32/32 rows, 1 live, 31 closed — at the cap, evicting on every open.** **(3) `MAX_REGISTRY_SESSIONS = 32` is unjustified at that value** (`registry.ts:58`). The documented reason is A5 accretion hygiene (`decisions/REGISTRY.md:281`), which is already handled independently by immediate-reaping rows that never ran a turn; the file is ~4 KB at 32 rows, so no size/latency argument survives contact (an earlier "the sweep would get slower" claim in this session was an unmeasured inference and is withdrawn). The number was also sized when the registry was the ONLY session list — post-SESSIONS-UNIFICATION history is unlimited and the registry is just the open+restorable layer, so a *count* cap may be the wrong shape vs recency. Operator framing: *"it's not a bookmark if it only goes to top when we open it"* — entry is an automatic side effect of looking, not a user choice. **Candidate fixes (not evaluated, not decided):** resolve the catalog entry before/at mint so the activity key never falls to click-time; don't mint a registry row for a browse-only open; raise or re-shape the cap. **#4 INTERACTION (2026-07-22, headless source analysis — GUI re-test owed):** the leading suspect (spawn-frozen / which-snapshot ambiguity → `byId.get(engineSessionId)` misses → row `createdAtMs`/`transcriptActivityAtMs` fall to `descriptor.createdAt`=click-instant at `sessionsCatalogState.ts:193,195`) is STRUCTURALLY ELIMINATED by catalog-owner #4: the merge now reads ONE continuously-refreshed main-owned catalog, so an opened session's on-disk transcript enters `byId` within ≤1 refresh (~30 s) and the row settles to real transcript-activity — vs pre-#4 "never settles". Residual transient: the row may still briefly warp until the ready frame binds `engineSessionId` AND the next catalog run lands (≤30 s). Fully killing the transient = the listed "resolve the entry at mint" fix (now #4-owned, NOT CC-2/parked; GUI-observable → operator sign-off). Re-test steps in `docs/migration/2026-07-22-phase4-gui-acceptance.md`. **⬜ FINDINGS (4)/(5) SUPERSEDED — see `reviews/2026-07-21-app-cutlist-ram-audit.md`.** This session independently found and hand-verified the 300 ms preview dwell-spawn (`App.tsx:2602-2613`, `PREVIEW_DWELL_MS = 300` at `previewTranscriptState.ts:42` → `engagePreview` → `restoreLiveSession` → host spawn, no user action) and the dead "Most active" sort (`messageCount` hardcoded 0 at `src/utils/sessionStorage.ts:5518`, never repaired by `enrichLog` `:5556-5572`, so `sortSessionRows` case `'active'` at `sessionsCatalogState.ts:318` always ties into its `modifiedAtMs` tiebreaker — byte-identical to `'recent'`). **The 07-21 audit covers BOTH with strictly better evidence and OWNS them** — Part I §1 (traced to `specs/2026-07-14-instant-session-open-design.md` M3, landed `f17853b`, spec self-declared NOT locked, dwell value = its open question Q2; cost measured ~885 MB vs this session's single `ps` sample of 862 MB) and Part I §7 (adds the spawn-frozen-catalog consequence: fresh sessions sort last AND have their count chip hidden). Its Part V decision queue + Part IV dispatch shape supersede any grouping proposed here. Recorded only so the two accounts are not mistaken for independent defects. **⬜ STILL UNIQUE TO THIS ROW (not covered by that audit, which is `app/`-scoped): (a)** the operator-observed stays-at-top warp above — the audit notes the activity-key duplication (its Part I §2) but not this live symptom or its cause; **(b)** the 32-row cap evicting genuinely-restorable rows on browse — the audit touches `MAX_REGISTRY_SESSIONS` only inside its idle-park design; **(c)** engine-side: accepting a workspace-trust prompt in a SUBDIRECTORY persists trust at the git root (`src/utils/config.ts:1625-1638` `getProjectPathForConfig` → `findCanonicalGitRoot`; read path walks ancestors at `:790-799`; app writes via `app/sidecar/workspaceTrustDomain.ts:73-79`), so trusting one package silently trusts every sibling package in the repo — found by review fan-out, source-anchored, NOT independently re-verified by the lead; **(d)** engine-side: a terminal `/rename` (`src/commands/rename/rename.ts:57`) writes only `customTitle`, while the desktop prefers the registry title unconditionally (`sessionsCatalogState.ts:258-262` `pickTitle`) and `broadcastSessionTitle` is documented as deliberately NOT part of attach/replay (`app/sidecar/sidecarServer.ts:2542`) — so a terminal rename never reaches the desktop and there is no repair path; same provenance/verification caveat as (c). |

The composer field is now a `contentEditable` div (`ComposerInput.tsx`), matching `Chat.jsx:1402`, so a collapsed paste renders as an atomic INLINE pill where its `[Pasted text #N]` token sits: clipboard glyph, `Pasted #N`, line/char count, `×` remove, accent tint (`Chat.jsx:1060-1097`). The out-of-band pill strip above the composer is DELETED, and the raw token is no longer visible as literal text in the field. New `composerDom.ts` owns the string↔DOM contract (`readComposerText` / `renderComposerDom` / selection-offset mapping / `buildPastePill`); the draft stays a plain controlled string, so the paste model, submit expansion, wire, and security baseline are untouched (renderer-only, no new frame/channel). `ComposerInputHandle` keeps the textarea-shaped `selectionStart`/`selectionEnd`/`setSelectionRange` API, so at-caret paste, whole-token Backspace, and edge-gated ↑/↓ recall are unchanged. Hover or caret-park on a pill opens the full-text popover headed `Pasted #N · N lines · N chars` (`Chat.jsx:1386`). Two behaviours a textarea gave for free are now explicit: small pastes are inserted as plain text (the default is always prevented, so clipboard HTML/images cannot enter the field), and ↑/↓ recall places the caret at the end of the recalled draft. The auto-resize effect is gone (a contentEditable grows natively). PARITY-LEDGER `:478`/`:484`/`:485`/`:499`/`:500`/`:501` updated; the three paste rows flip 🔁 adapted → ✅ built. **COLD-REVIEW REWORK 2026-08-05 (`89bdce0`; review found 4 real defects in `b6846e0`, three of them behaviours a contentEditable does NOT inherit from the textarea it replaced):** **F1 (high, would lose user text)** — a pill's DOM listeners are attached once and deliberately survive later keystrokes (that is why the nodes are kept), so the remove callback held the draft AS IT WAS when the pill was built: paste, type after it, click `×`, and everything typed since the paste was overwritten. Listeners now read live refs (`onRemovePasteRef`/`pastesRef`) and locate their own occurrence from the live DOM. **F2** — token SYNTAX was treated as proof of backing content: typing the literal characters `[Pasted text #9]` produced a pill with an empty preview and a dead `×`, and a duplicated token gave two pills over one entry where removing either could strand the other. `splitComposerText` now takes a `hasEntry` predicate (unbacked tokens stay literal text and merge into the surrounding run); `removePasteOccurrence` cuts the CLICKED occurrence; the ordinary `'edit'` prune decides the stored text's fate, so a surviving duplicate still expands. **F3** — Alt/Meta+Enter relied on the browser's default, but a contentEditable answers it with a block container while the serializer emits a newline only for `<br>`, so the break rendered once then vanished from the draft (contradicting ledger `:483`). The newline is now written into the draft directly. **F4** — a rich drag-and-drop bypassed the plain-text normalization paste already had, leaving foreign DOM in a field whose draft is a plain string. `onDrop` now takes the plain text and places it at the drop point. **F5** — the battery figure below is re-measured in a CLEAN CLONE; the earlier `2679` was contaminated by concurrent work on the shared tree, and the claim that `composerDom.test.ts` covered the pill's shape was wrong (it exercises the pure string↔DOM contract only). **Battery 2026-08-05, clean clone at `89bdce0`:** `bun test app/` **2697/0** (direct parent `bc3eaa0` = 2690, so this commit adds exactly **+7**; `b6846e0` = 2669 vs its parent `6971cde` = 2653, +16) · app tsc clean · sidecar wrapper 0-owned (5560 upstream ignored) · `renderer:build` clean, every pill/popover class emitted in the built CSS (Tailwind trap re-checked) · hardening **19/19**. **⬜ operator GUI acceptance — this is the load-bearing gate, and the review confirmed why: the renderer suite is SSR-only, so the field builds its content in a layout effect no headless test can run, and NO DOM-capable harness exists in this package** (adding one is a dependency decision, not this session's call). The four review defects are covered only as far as pure logic reaches: `splitComposerText`'s backing-entry rule and `removePasteOccurrence`'s occurrence/prune semantics are unit-tested, and the three DOM-side behaviours (live-ref removal, authored newline, normalized drop) are pinned STRUCTURALLY against the source so a later edit cannot silently hand them back to the browser. That is a tripwire, not proof they work. Steps: paste >800 chars or >2 newlines → an inline pill appears at the caret, not raw token text; hover it → preview popover; **type after the pill, then click `×` → only the pill goes and the typing survives (F1)**; paste the literal text `[Pasted text #9]` → stays plain text, no pill (F2); **Alt+Enter and Meta+Enter → the newline persists after the next keystroke (F3)**; drag selected rich text in from a browser → plain text only, no styling or links (F4); Backspace right after a pill → whole pill goes; ↑↓-recall around a pill → caret behaves; submit → the engine receives the full pasted text. **CORRECTION 2026-08-09:** the historical honest-toast/no-wire-attachment-capability statements above are superseded by the P4-0 correction dated 2026-08-09. The current composer has a real one-image attachment path; this row otherwise remains historical, and its GUI acceptance remains pending. **✅ IMAGE REVIEW FOLLOW-UP 2026-08-09:** accepted files are capped at 25 MiB before `FileReader`, files whose base64 cannot fit the submit budget skip that allocation and enter compression directly, a synchronous in-flight ref prevents overlapping paste preparations from releasing the submit gate early, submit stays disabled until preparation settles, and unsupported clipboard image types reach visible feedback. Direct preparation tests cover pre-read rejection, passthrough and compression; App source tripwires cover the interaction because the package remains SSR-only. | **P4-33** Composer rail + transcript + shell residue (CC-1 §6 · §12 · §5 · §1 · §2 · §4) | CLAUDE (visual-design) 🖐 GUI | 6 | 🟡 **CODE-COMPLETE + headless GREEN 2026-07-30** · ✅ cold review NO BLOCKERS · ✅ **MERGED 2026-07-30** (`3ce2afe`, pushed to `origin/migration`) · ⬜ operator GUI acceptance PENDING · ⬜ 5 §0 proposals awaiting a ruling | **TRANCHE G — merged 2026-07-30 (`3ce2afe`) after a delay worth recording.** It was the only Tranche G branch touching `app/renderer/src/TranscriptView.tsx`(+`.test.tsx`), which another live session held UNCOMMITTED on `migration` for the whole batch; git refuses a merge that would overwrite local changes, and stashing or committing another session's in-flight work is forbidden (CLAUDE.md §4), so it was parked rather than forced. The operator confirmed that session had finished, its work was committed on its behalf (`16340b8`), and the merge then went through with `TranscriptView` **auto-merging cleanly** — the prediction that the two sides did not overlap textually held. **Two conflicts did surface elsewhere, both resolved keeping both sides:** in `App.tsx` the session-actions target now carries BOTH P4-29's `fromSessionsPage` and this session's `origin`, and git had misaligned this session's rename fields against P4-30's export-dialog block, so `origin` was reattached to `setRenamingSession` where it belongs (a blind resolution would have put it on the wrong call); in `theme.css` both keyframes and both utilities were kept. **Integration fix the merge exposed:** this session made `origin` required, which P4-29's Sessions-page call site predates, so `SessionActionsOrigin` gained a third `sessions-page` member rather than mislabelling that entry point a tab — the rail-pin check is `=== 'sidebar'`, so the new member correctly does not pin. Post-merge battery on `migration`: `bun test app/` **1994 pass / 0 fail**, app tsc clean, sidecar zero owned, hardening **19/19**, `renderer:build` ok, and both `animate-sa-pop` and `animate-token-warn-in` verified present in built CSS. **Row count: 3 built · 5 §0 proposals · 5 deferred.** Built: `:929` **TokenWarning** (the composer rail's amber auto-compact glyph + popover), with BOTH thresholds shipped from the sidecar over an additive outbound `RunControlsSnapshot.autoCompact` because neither is renderer-derivable (the denominator is `getEffectiveContextWindowSize`, not the gauge's window, and the buffer is model-dependent) — copying the prototype's flat 13k would print a percentage disagreeing with the engine that actually compacts; `:246` sidebar menu guard (🔁 adapted: the ⋯ state is SHARED with the TabBar, so App now records the raising surface and pins only for sidebar-anchored overlays); `:372` UserBubble copy chip + ➕ `group-focus-within` (the prototype is hover-only, hence keyboard-unreachable) + the prototype's copy toast. **Two premises in this session's own dispatch block were WRONG, verified against source and independently confirmed in cold review:** (1) `shouldDefer` on `EnterPlanModeTool` is deferred tool-SCHEMA loading (`src/Tool.ts:446-450`), NOT permission approval — the tool flips mode directly inside `call()` and mints no request; (2) the prototype's `compact` prop is DEAD (`WorkspaceLayout.jsx:218` passes it, `Chat.jsx:317` destructures it, never read), so there was no density spec to port. **`:494` composer command routing was BUILT, then REVERTED after review** — narrowing it to "commands with no engine implementation" turned out to be the EMPTY SET: every routable name has a real `src/commands/` implementation, and the two map keys without one are unreachable because the composer picker is catalog-gated to what the live engine reports. Shipping it would have traded engine behavior for navigation on every command (picking `/doctor` would navigate to Settings and never run `/doctor`). | **P4-35** `starting` renders as a red failure in `ConnectionRecovery` (audit 24a) | ANY | 2 | ✅ **DONE 2026-07-31** | **Contract:** `starting` is the renderer's projection of the ONE send-failure code the wire marks `retryable: true` — `session_not_ready`, minted by `sendFailureCodeForStatus` only while the child is still `spawning`/`connecting` (`app/supervisor/supervisor.ts:509`; `SidecarSendError.retryable` `:127`; `ErrorFrame.retryable` `app/shared/protocol.ts:564`) — so it is transient by construction and must not paint a failure bar. **Derivation:** (1) producer is an **error** frame, not a lifecycle frame (`connectionState.ts:64-83`); (2) the existing classifier is `resolvePendingSubmit` (`composerState.ts:513-536`), which partitions the same union and `release`s exactly `dead`/`disconnected`/`failed`/`exited` — the new guard **aligns**, it does not deviate (`selectComposerGate`'s `connectPending` `:409-411` and `restoreConnecting` `App.tsx:3264-3266` group `connecting`+`starting` the same way); (3) `starting` is bounded — the spawn ends in `ready`, or in a terminal lifecycle frame via the F12 socket-wait cap (200 × 50 ms ≈ 10 s → kill + `failed`, `supervisor.ts:376-395`), `child.on('error')`→`failed` `:252`, `child.on('exit')`→`exited` `:264`; the ONE unbounded hole is a sidecar that connects its socket and then goes silent forever without exiting (no ready-frame handshake timeout after `setStatus(record,'connecting')`), and the composer's parked-prompt path already waits indefinitely in that same hole, so this changes no risk. **Fix:** new shared partition `isTerminalConnectionStatus` (`connectionState.ts:55`, own exhaustiveness tripwire) consumed by the guard (`App.tsx:4028`). Agreement with `resolvePendingSubmit` is pinned by test, so the two lists cannot drift. **Tests:** `App.test.tsx:1061` now covers all seven union members + the null-session case (proven red-then-green by reverting the guard); `connectionState.test.ts:158,165` add the partition + agreement tests with a compile-time completeness record. **Battery:** `bun test app/` 1996 pass / 0 fail (baseline 1994 + 2) · `bunx tsc --noEmit -p app/tsconfig.json` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` passed (5542 upstream ignored, zero owned) · `test:hardening` 19/19. **OUT as fenced:** the raw status word in the copy (26c class) and any connection tone grammar (24b, CC-5 ruling #6). No §0 deviations. Ledger §12 row "ConnectionChip: hidden when healthy" re-anchored. |

| **P4-43** The permission card's advertised keyboard shortcuts are dead (audit 12) | ANY 🖐 GUI | 4 | 🟡 headless green 2026-07-31, operator GUI pending (commits `2df63f8`, `b147750`) | **Built inside the fence: `PermissionPrompt.tsx`, `PermissionQueue.tsx`, `permissionPromptModel.ts`, the permission-keyboard region of `App.tsx`, and the two colocated test files. Nothing else in `App.tsx` was entered.** **Contract the fix satisfies:** the keyboard answers the SAME engine-minted request id the buttons do, because it calls the identical `allowPermission`/`denyPermission` path keyed on `selectVisiblePermission`'s pick (`permissionState.ts:267-282`), and the card that takes focus is derived from that same value, so a card can never advertise keys the listener does not deliver (T5a untouched: the renderer still never mints a request id). **The trap is CONFIRMED, not refuted.** `PermissionPrompt.tsx:90` renders `role="alertdialog"` on the card's own `<section>`, that role is in the guard's selector, and `closest()` matches the element itself — so the prototype's mechanism ported literally (focus a `tabIndex={-1}` card) leaves all four keys exactly as dead as leaving focus in the composer. It is also the ONLY `role="alertdialog"` in production renderer source (swept), which is why the selector entry could not simply be dropped without weakening the general rule. **The fix is therefore two parts, and neither works alone:** (1) the target card takes focus on appear and hands it back on unmount if it still holds it (`PermissionPrompt.tsx:104-124`, the `PlanPanel.tsx:99-110` precedent); (2) that card carries `data-permission-key-host`, and the guard is now one exported predicate, `permissionKeysAreLive` (`permissionPromptModel.ts:24-78`), which treats the marked host as hosting the keys rather than owning them. **Listener ordering: unchanged, because the listener did not move.** It stays a `document` listener, and `dedicatedFlowOwnsKeyboard` (`App.tsx:2001-2003`) is untouched, so the double-resolve it prevents cannot come back — propagation is still target → document → window, and while `AskQuestionFlow` or `PlanPanel` hold their `window` listeners this one is not attached at all. The new focus target is gated on the SAME flag, so no generic card steals focus or advertises keys underneath a question or a plan. **P4-42's new disclosure button:** `button` is in the selector, so while focus sits on `Show input` / `Hide input` the keys are genuinely dead — and the hint now disappears in exactly that state rather than lying. Same for the Deny/Allow/always-allow buttons and, per the prompt's constraint, the deny-feedback `<input>`, where typing the letter `n` must never deny. Clicking back onto the card body returns focus to nothing, where the keys were always live. **Hint honesty:** the strip renders only while `keyboardTarget && keysLive && !denyOnly` (`:126`, `:253-257`) — one card at a time, never a card the shortcuts skip, never a state where a control owns the key. Renderer-only: no frame kind, no preload method, no protocol change, no new dep, security baseline untouched. Evidence: `bun test app/` **2154 pass / 0 fail** (161 files) · `bunx tsc --noEmit -p app/tsconfig.json` clean · `typecheck:sidecar` scoped pass (5,542 upstream ignored, 0 owned) · `test:hardening` **19/19** · `renderer:build` ok · `lint:fast-refresh` clean. 2 tests added to `PermissionPrompt.test.tsx`; the `FIX-5 keyboard tripwire` in `App.test.tsx` was re-pointed at the predicate's new home and gained three call-site pins, and its own LAYER-HONESTY note asked for exactly this move (predicate into a `.ts` module). **§0 flags (proposals, OPEN and unapproved):** (a) ➕ real-added — focus is RESTORED to the previously focused element when the card leaves, which the prototype does not do; without it, answering by keyboard would drop focus on the floor and the user would have to click back into the composer; (b) 🔁 adapted — the hint is conditional, where the prototype's footer strip is unconditional, because the prototype's card is a modal portal with no rival focus owners while this card sits inline with a live composer; (c) ⚠️ hazard the operator should judge live, NOT a scope change — the card now takes focus from the composer mid-turn, and the composer stays editable during a turn (`composerState.ts:414`), so a user typing a queued message when a card appears has focus pulled away, and a subsequent `n` or Enter answers the card. The prototype behaves the same way. The only signal is the key-hint strip appearing; no focus ring was invented; (d) considered and REJECTED as bound-exceeding: letting a focused BUTTON keep only Enter while `n`/⌫/Esc pass through, which would widen the live-key surface beyond "make the advertised keys work from the state the user is actually in". **The composer's autofocus was NOT changed** (decided, per the fence: the fix works without touching it). **Only the operator's GUI run can settle:** that focus actually lands on the card (SSR suite has no DOM, no focus, no effects); that each of Enter / N / ⌫ / Esc reaches the handler and resolves the right request; that typing `n` in the deny field does not deny; that one Enter with an `AskUserQuestion` up answers only the question; that the hint appears and disappears as focus moves, including onto P4-42's disclosure button; and whether the focus steal reads as helpful or as theft. | **P4-61** Sidebar rebuilt from the operator's Claude Design source (`Cat Code Sidebar`, `components/sidebar/index.html`) | CLAUDE (visual-design) | 5 | 🟡 **headless + static-render green 2026-08-01, operator GUI pending** | Operator-directed, not backlog-generated. **Built:** a `New chat` action above the list; a `Pinned` section that LIFTS a pinned session out of its project group (never duplicated) with its own manual, drag/⌥↑⌥↓-reorderable order; a `Projects` section header whose `+` is add-project (the native picker — the session created in the chosen folder is what makes the group appear, since the model has no empty-workspace record); per-row pin plus the existing ⋮ in an overlay that only takes the row's right edge under the pointer, so titles get full width at rest; workspace headers restyled to open/closed folder glyphs with a hover-revealed `+`; and a footer carrying the active account (avatar + alias, linking to Accounts) with the five destinations folded behind a toggle that unfolds them upward. The COLLAPSED rail still shows all five destinations as icons under the account glyph, which is what lets the expanded footer keep them folded. **Pins are renderer-local** (`sidebarPinnedSessions.ts`, the `sidebarWorkspaceOrder.ts` idiom verbatim: versioned JSON under `catcode.sidebarPinnedSessions.v1`, injectable storage, capped, best-effort) — keyed on `MergedSessionRow.sessionId`, the ENGINE id both a registry and a terminal-history row carry, so terminal sessions are pinnable too. No protocol frame, preload channel, registry field or host verb was added; `New chat` and the group `+` both route through the existing `createSessionInWorkspace(repId)` (HC1/T8: a REGISTRY id, never a renderer-authored cwd) and add-project through the existing picker path. **§0 flags:** ➕ KEPT the `time · model` subtitle and the O1 live dot, which the design source lacks only because its sample data carries no timestamps or models — dropping shipped information on that reading would be a silent cut; 🔁 `New chat` opens a session in the ACTIVE workspace with no picker, falling back to the picker when nothing is open, since the design source leaves it unwired and routing it to the picker too would give the rail two identical buttons. **A real defect was caught by looking at the render, not by the suite:** the actions backing painted at rest on a pinned row (whose pin stays visible so an unpin is not a hover-hunt), drawing a lit rectangle over an unlit row; the backing is now hover/focus-only, matching the design source, and lives in `theme.css` because a multi-stop gradient cannot be an interpolated Tailwind class and a plain class cannot take a `group-hover:` variant. **Session rows inside a project are drag-reorderable too** (operator, 2026-08-02: "we can drag the session in the claude design but still cant in app"), which needed the manual-vs-CC-2 conflict resolved rather than left to fight: `sidebarSessionOrder.ts` namespaces the order BY cwd (a session's project IS its cwd, so a cross-project drop is refused rather than faked), a ranked row holds its slot so a message no longer floats it, and UNRANKED rows sit ABOVE the hand-arranged block in activity order so a brand-new session lands on top instead of being buried under the shelf. A group nobody has dragged is byte-for-byte today's activity order, so the feature is invisible until used. The first drag in a group freezes that group's whole sequence, otherwise every untouched row would jump the arrangement just made. All three reorder gestures in the rail (workspace headers, Pinned, project rows) now share one drop-edge rule, one keyboard path (⌥↑/⌥↓) and one row-handler shape (`RowReorderHandlers`), and are kept apart by four distinct drag payload types (`text/workspace-cwd`, `text/pinned-session-id`, `text/sidebar-session-id`, and the tab-split's `text/sessionId`) so none lights up another's drop edges. **A project can now be REMOVED from the rail too** (operator, 2026-08-02: "we have a way to add the projects but what still missing is a way to remove it"): each project header carries a hover ⋮ whose one-row menu hides that group, and the Projects section grows a "Show N hidden projects" line that brings them all back. Hiding is a VIEW state, not a delete, because a project is derived and not stored: `groupByWorkspace` buckets the roster by cwd and Add-project only opens the picker, so there is no project record to delete, and no delete verb exists anywhere (`sessionActions.ts` returns tag/archive/delete as CUT). `sidebarHiddenWorkspaces.ts` is the `sidebarPinnedSessions.ts` idiom verbatim (versioned JSON under `catcode.sidebarHiddenWorkspaces.v1`, injectable storage, capped, best-effort), keyed on cwd, and each entry stores WHEN it was hidden so a project with newer CC-2 activity un-hides itself: without that, picking a hidden project's folder from Add-project would create a session into an invisible group and the picker would look broken. Hidden groups keep their rank in `allGroups`, so unhiding drops them back into their arranged slot. The menu is deliberately NOT `SessionActionsMenu` (that union is per-SESSION verbs; only the placement and panel shape are shared). No protocol frame, preload channel, registry field or host verb was added.
